# LUMI research log, kernel release copy

Research log of the LUMI project EHPC-DEV-2026D11-419: kernel, baseline, scaling and compiler entries.
Edited for publication: run-management notes removed.
Paths under `evidence/` name the raw-output archive, which is not part of this release, and so do a few helper
scripts that entries name but `make_release.sh` does not copy (for example `scaling/vllm_mn_dump.sbatch` and
`scaling/hangdump/`). Paths that start with `scripts/` name the working directories on LUMI; the matching files
are in `kernels/`, `baselines/`, `scaling/` and `experiments/`; the source behind each entry is
in `experiments/`. md5 values refer to the research copies of the files listed in `kernels/` and `experiments/`, before
`make_release.sh` replaced the project path in experiment scripts and reworded a few kernel comments; the kernel files
whose release bytes differ are listed with both md5 values in `kernels/RELEASE-EDITS.md`.

## Common setup (unless an entry says otherwise)

- **Hardware:** LUMI-G node, one GCD of an AMD MI250X (gfx90a), dev-g partition.
  - Per GCD: 110 CUs, 64 KB LDS per workgroup, HBM ~1.6 TB/s, fp16 MFMA peak ~191.5 TFLOPS.
- **Software:** container `lumi-pytorch-rocm-6.2.4-python-3.12-pytorch-v2.7.1.sif`, PyTorch 2.7.1+rocm6.2.4, TileLang 0.1.15
  (PyPI wheel, tag a35f8ddf), hipcc wrapper with a gfx90a shim (`__builtin_amdgcn_wavefrontsize`, FNUZ FP8 types).
- **Correctness:** relative L2 error vs a float64 (attention) or float32 (MoE) reference.
- **Timing:** `tilelang.profiler.do_bench` (warmup 10–20, rep 50–100).

---

### 2026-11-04 · Setup and first checks
- **TileLang GEMM:** 70/70 configs correct.
  - Best vs hipBLASLt: 0.73–0.92× on large shapes; 0.35× at M = 64 (decode shape).
- **Upstream AMD unit tests:** 59 pass, 10 fail. All 10 failures are int8/FP8 instructions that gfx90a lacks; the tests are missing gfx90a skips.
- **Upstream flash-attention example:** "fails" on d128 (every output element ~5% off), and d256 fails to launch.
- **vLLM 0.30.0 (ROCm 7.2.3 SIF):** serves Qwen3.8-27B BF16 on TP = 2.
  - Speed: 33 tok/s at 1 user, 649 tok/s at 32.
  - Fixes needed: spawn workers, no explicit `/dev/shm` bind.

### 2026-11-05 · Is the TileLang attention kernel wrong on gfx90a? (No: the reference is.)
- **Question:** where does the ~5% error come from?
- **Method 1** (`fa_diag.py`): check each step alone on one block.
  - Steps: Q·Kᵀ, row max/sum on the MFMA fragment, P·V with P in registers, and P·V with P in shared memory.
  - Result: all ≤ 3e-5 across 6 tile/thread/k_pack configs.
- **Method 2** (`fa_diag2.py`): sweep the sequence length.
  - Result: error grows 0.07% (256) → 0.3% (1k) → 4.4% (4k), spread evenly over rows; it isn't a scale factor.
- **Method 3** (`fa_diag3.py`): compare everything to float64.

  | | 1k tokens | 4k tokens |
  |---|---|---|
  | TileLang kernel | 0.028% | 0.028% |
  | torch SDPA | 0.028% | 0.028% |
  | the example's fp16 `ref_program` | 0.33% | 4.3% |

  Rasterization and split settings change nothing.
- **Conclusion:** the kernel is correct. The example's fp16 einsum + softmax reference drifts on ROCm. Upstream issue to file: compute the reference in fp32.
- **d256 launch failures:** caused by LDS. (bM + 2·bN)·D·2 B = 64 KB fails; 48 KB works.

### 2026-11-05 · Attention prefill: upstream example tiles vs torch SDPA (`fa_bench.py`)
- d128, 8 heads, 4k tokens: best 0.90× SDPA (43.5 vs 48.0 TF).
- d256, GQA 6 (24 q / 4 kv heads), causal, 4k tokens: best 0.63× SDPA (34.0 vs 53.7 TF).

### 2026-11-05 · GQA-packed prefill kernel (`gqa_fa.py`)
- **Idea:** one workgroup takes P query positions × all G query heads of one kv head.
  - That gives P·G GEMM rows, and each K/V tile is loaded once instead of G times.
  - Q is kept in registers, so LDS holds only K and V.
- **Sweep at 4k:**
  - Q in shared memory or few warps: 2–4× slower.
  - Best: P = 32 (192 rows), bN = 16, 768 threads, k_pack 2, 16 KB LDS.
- **Results** (error vs float64 2.5e-4–2.6e-4 everywhere):

  | tokens | SDPA TF | TileLang TF | ratio |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1k | 24.5 | 36.3 | 1.48× |
  | 4k | 53.8 | 65.8 | 1.22× |
  | 8k | 63.1 | 74.6 | 1.18× |

- 74.6 TF is ~39% of fp16 peak.
- Not measured: 16k (the float64 reference alone needs ~50 GB), batch > 1, CK FMHA, d128 GQA.

### 2026-11-05 · GQA flash-decoding (`gqa_decode.py`)
- **Design:** grid (splits, B·Hkv).
  - A block takes one kv head, its 6 query heads (padded to a 16-row MFMA tile) and one slice of the cache.
  - It writes partial O + LSE; a second kernel combines them.
  - Variable sequence lengths are supported.
- **Round 1:** bN 32, 64 threads (one wavefront). Exact, but only 8–13% of HBM.
  - FullCol policy with 128/256 threads fails TileLang layout inference on a 16-row tile.
- **Round 2 (negative):** a SIMT variant (no MFMA, 256–512 threads, dot products in T.Parallel + serial loops) was 10–50× slower; TileLang lowers it poorly.
- **Round 3:** bN 16 (16 KB LDS, so 4 blocks per CU) with more splits.

  | B × L | GB/s | % HBM | vs SDPA |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 × 4k | 156 | 9.7 | 5.7× |
  | 1 × 131k | 468 | 29.2 | 16× |
  | 8 × 32k | 508 | 31.8 | 5.3× |
  | 32 × 8k | 538 | 33.6 | 5.0× |

  Error vs float64 2.4e-4–2.9e-4. SDPA (`enable_gqa`) is weak at decode on ROCm (30–110 GB/s), so % of HBM is the fairer metric.
- **Conclusion:** occupancy (LDS per block) was the limit; memory-level parallelism is next.

GPU use on 5 Nov: ~1.5 GPU-h (dev-g, 1 GCD jobs).

### 2026-11-05 · Fused MoE grouped GEMM (`tilelang/moe_fused.py`) vs vLLM 0.30 fused_experts (`tilelang/vllm_moe_baseline.py`)
- **Shape:** H 2048, I 512, E 256, top-8, fp16. (This is the Qwen3.6-35B-A3B MoE shape as recalled; verify against the config before publishing.)
- **Baseline:** vLLM 0.30.0 `fused_experts` (Triton, ROCm 7.2.3 SIF, torch 2.12). Error vs float32 5.2e-4–5.5e-4.

  | tokens | 1 | 16 | 64 | 256 | 1,024 | 4,096 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | vLLM ms | 0.328 | 0.577 | 1.397 | 1.799 | 2.240 | 3.851 |

- **Design:**
  - Align: sort the (token, expert) pairs by expert and pad each group to bM.
  - Kernel 1: gather X rows, then X·W_gateᵀ and X·W_upᵀ, then SiLU(gate)·up in one kernel.
  - Kernel 2: Inter·W_downᵀ times the routing weight, written to Y[pair]; then a torch top-k sum.
- **Round 1** (align in torch): error 3.1e-4. The two kernels alone were already fast at T = 1 (0.13 ms), but the torch align added ~0.5 ms (many small ops plus a host sync for the row count).
- **Round 2** (GPU align, one thread per expert scanning all pairs, fixed-size grid): align 16 µs at T = 1 but 3.7 ms at T = 4,096. Empty blocks still computed.
- **Round 3** (shared-memory atomics in align, `atomic_add(..., return_prev=True)`; empty blocks exit): align 13–180 µs.
  - Pair order inside an expert is no longer fixed. Each row is computed independently and written to its own pair index, so the output doesn't depend on it.
- **Round 4** (bigger tiles, k_pack 2): no gain. Best large-T tile stays bM 64, bN 64, bK 64, 256 threads, down bN 128.
- **Round 5 (negative):** pre-gathering X into expert order with a torch gather was slower. It also copies the padding rows, up to 16k rows at T = 1.
- **Best so far vs vLLM** (error 3.1e-4–3.2e-4 vs float32 everywhere):

  | tokens | ours ms | vLLM ms | ratio | tile |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | 0.115 | 0.328 | **2.85×** | bM 16, bN 16, bK 128, 64 thr |
  | 16 | 0.681 | 0.577 | 0.85× | same |
  | 64 | 1.416 | 1.397 | 0.99× | same |
  | 256 | 1.856 | 1.799 | 0.97× | same |
  | 1,024 | 2.787 | 2.240 | 0.80× | bM 64, bN 64, bK 64, 256 thr |
  | 4,096 | 6.30 | 3.851 | 0.61× | same |

- **Breakdown at 4,096:** align 0.14, gate/up 3.17 (43 TF), down 2.56 (27 TF), top-k sum 0.52 ms.
  - The large-batch gap is GEMM efficiency on gfx90a: no async copy, and `num_stages > 1` falls back to a sequential loop on HIP. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** "no async copy" is wrong: the hardware has a direct load, CK's binaries contain it, TileLang gates it; not yet shown running by us. "The gap is GEMM efficiency" is also unsupported: vLLM's k1 compiled at num_stages 2 has no overlap either, and the gap is only partly attributed (`writeup/vendor-kernel-analysis.md` sections 4 and 5.7).
  - It ties to Track 4 (dense GEMM 0.73–0.92× hipBLASLt).
- **Open:** fuse the top-k sum into kernel 2 (atomics or a per-token reduction), GEMM pipelining by hand (double-buffered LDS), and smaller-T bandwidth (vLLM reaches 1,100 GB/s at T = 16; we reach ~930).

GPU use for MoE today: ~1.2 GPU-h.

### 2026-11-05 · Dense GEMM: overlap variants and small-M split-K (`tilelang/gemm_prefetch.py`)
- **Question:** gfx90a has no async global→LDS copy, and TileLang's `T.Pipelined(num_stages > 1)` falls back to a sequential loop on HIP. Can hand-written overlap close the gap to hipBLASLt? **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** the premise is wrong: the hardware has a direct load, CK's binaries contain it, TileLang gates it; not yet shown running by us. The `num_stages` fallback is a separate gate, and explicit `T.Pipelined(order, stage)` is not stripped (compile only).
- **Variants** (fp16, C = A·Bᵀ, error 2.1e-4 vs float32 everywhere):
  - `regpref`: next tile global→registers before the MFMAs, registers→LDS after.
  - `dbuf`: two LDS buffers.

  | shape | hipBLASLt TF | base 128×128×32 | dbuf 128×128×32 | base 128×128×64 | regpref best |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4096³ | 102.8 | 0.82× | 0.87× | **0.94×** | 0.47× |
  | 8192³ | 106.9 | 0.67× | **0.85×** | 0.79× | 0.40× |
  | 4096×17408×5120 | 106.7 | 0.72× | 0.73× | **0.82×** | 0.39× |
  | 32768×1024×2048 (MoE expert-like) | 93.9 | 1.01× | 0.94× | **1.06×** | 0.49× |

- **Findings:**
  - Register prefetch is a negative result: 3–4× slower, because TileLang's fragment layout for the staging copies is poor. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** the cause is scratch memory, not fragment layout. The staging arrays stay in scratch (ScratchSize 144 B/lane at bK 64), so no load overlaps math, and this never tested register staging. In one unrepeated run, scalar registers took `regstage` 128×128×64 at 4096³ from 25.21 to 102.56 TF (`writeup/vendor-kernel-analysis.md` section 5.2).
  - Double buffering helps only with bK 32; with bK 64 it doubles LDS and costs occupancy.
  - 256×128 tiles are worse everywhere (~0.5×). **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** the cause is register occupancy (Total 272, Occupancy 1), not the tile. In one unrepeated run, `amdgpu_waves_per_eu(2)` gave 84.10, 98.12 and 97.54 TF at 4096³, 8192³ and 32768×1024×2048. All variants shared one source file, so a single-kernel compile may differ (section 5.3 of the same writeup).
- **Small M** (decode, `gemm_splitk`): grid (N/bN, M/bM, S), fp32 atomics into a zeroed buffer, then cast. Timing includes the zeroing and the cast. Error 2.1e-4.

  | M×N×K | hipBLASLt ms | best TileLang ms | ratio | config |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1×5120×5120 | 0.0654 | 0.0692 | 0.95× | bN 64, bK 128, S 4 |
  | 32×5120×5120 | 0.0708 | 0.0830 | 0.85× | bN 64, bK 64, S 4 |
  | 64×5120×5120 | 0.0846 | 0.1034 | 0.82× | bN 64, bK 64, S 4 |
  | 64×5120×17408 | 0.2432 | 0.2262 | **1.08×** | bN 64, bK 64, S 4 |
  | 16×17408×5120 | 0.1922 | 0.2439 | 0.79× | bN 64, bK 128, S 4 |
  | 64×17408×5120 | 0.2121 | 0.2746 | 0.77× | bN 64, bK 64, S 1 |

  Before split-K, M = 64 was 0.35–0.45×. The Track 4 small-M target (≥ 0.8×) is met on 4 of 6 shapes. The S = 1 case still pays for the zeroing and the cast (to remove).

### 2026-11-05 · Attention prefill vs Composable Kernel (flash-attn 2.8.3) + remaining Track 1 shapes
- **Baseline** (`tilelang/ck_fa_baseline.py`): flash-attn 2.8.3, ROCm build (CK backend; `FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE` unset), in the vLLM 0.30.0 ROCm 7.2.3 SIF (torch 2.12), native GQA. Error vs float64 3.9e-4–4.5e-4.
- **aiter:** importing it in a GPU job hung until the 20-min job limit (probably a JIT build for gfx90a). Left opt-in (`TRY_AITER=1`); not measured.
- **TileLang GQA kernel** (`gqa_fa.py`, P 32, bN 16, 768 threads, Q in registers; d128: bN 32). Error 2.5e-4 vs float64 (≤ 8k), or 1.2e-4 vs SDPA (16k, batch 4).

  | case | SDPA (torch 2.7.1/ROCm 6.2) TF | CK flash-attn TF | TileLang TF | ÷ SDPA | ÷ CK |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | d256 GQA6 causal 1k | 24.5 | 52.9 | 36.3 | 1.48× | 0.69× |
  | d256 GQA6 causal 4k | 53.8 | 84.2 | 65.8 | 1.22× | 0.78× |
  | d256 GQA6 causal 4k, batch 4 | 59.2 | – | 72.6 | 1.23× | – |
  | d256 GQA6 causal 8k | 63.1 | 92.4 | 74.6 | 1.18× | 0.81× |
  | d256 GQA6 causal 16k | 65.4 | 96.2 | 74.4 | 1.14× | 0.77× |
  | d128 GQA6 causal 4k (bN 32) | 57.3 | 96.6 | 63.0 | 1.10× | 0.65× |
  | d128 MHA 4k non-causal (upstream tile, earlier run) | 48.0 | 85.8 | 43.5 | 0.90× | 0.51× |

- **Conclusions:**
  - The SDPA comparison mostly measures PyTorch 2.7.1/ROCm 6.2's attention path, which is not CK. The vendor-tuned reference is CK flash-attn: **1.2–2× faster than our TileLang kernels** on every shape.
  - Track 1's "≥ 1.2× SDPA" is met for d256 at 1k, at 4k, and at 4k with batch 4. It is missed at 8k (1.18×), 16k (1.14×) and d128 (1.10×).
  - Paper framing: TileLang reaches 65–81% of CK on gfx90a for GQA attention. The remaining gap is plausibly the missing software pipelining (no async copy; sequential `num_stages` on HIP). That is a hypothesis to test with a CK-style LDS double buffer plus register prefetch, which needs layout control TileLang 0.1.15 doesn't give us. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** "no async copy" is wrong: the hardware has a direct load, CK's binaries contain it, TileLang gates it; not yet shown running by us. CK uses it for K and register staging for V. The gap is still unattributed: instruction overhead with many waves is a competing explanation, and neither is measured (section 5.6 of the same writeup).
- **Errata:** blog 1 and the abstract must not imply "faster than vendor attention". Corrected the same day.

### 2026-11-05 · Decode vs vendor kernels, paged KV, and reaching > 50% of HBM (`tilelang/gqa_decode.py`, `ck_decode_baseline.py`, `vllm_paged_baseline.py`)
- **Baselines** (Qwen GQA d256, 24 q / 4 kv heads, one new token, error 2.8e-4–2.9e-4 vs float64):
  - CK flash-attn 2.8.3 `flash_attn_with_kvcache` (split-KV, contiguous cache): 37–38 GB/s at batch 1, 263 GB/s at 8 × 32k, 506 GB/s (31.6%) at 32 × 8k. Its paged mode needs page sizes divisible by 128 (vLLM uses 16); with page 256 it's ~10% slower.
  - vLLM 0.30 `paged_attention_rocm`: "Unsupported head size: 256". On ROCm, vLLM therefore runs Qwen-style d256 decode through its Triton unified-attention kernel.
  - vLLM Triton `unified_attention` (vLLM's NHD page layout, page 16): 49 GB/s at batch 1, 334 GB/s at 8 × 32k, 924 GB/s (57.7%) at 32 × 8k, 951 GB/s (59.4%) at 64 × 4k.
- **Ours, paged (vLLM NHD layout, page 16 = one tile, pages shuffled through the block table):**
  - Round 1 (64 threads, rows padded to 16): 31–39% of HBM.
  - Padded rows (round 4/5): pad the 6 query heads to bM 32 rows with 128 threads (two wavefronts share each K/V tile; the extra MFMA work is free in a bandwidth-bound kernel), and use 8–24 blocks per CU. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** 8–24 is launched blocks ÷ 110 CUs (`gqa_decode.py:325-327`), not blocks resident at once. LDS is 2 × 16 × 256 × 2 = 16384 B per block (read from source, not from a compile), so a 64 KB per-CU pool holds at most 4 at a time, which agrees with line 77.

  | B × L | splits | ours GB/s | % HBM | vLLM Triton | CK flash-attn | ours ÷ vLLM |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 × 131k | 220 | 762 | 47.6 | 49 | 38 | 15.5× |
  | 8 × 32k | 55 | 916 | **57.2** | 334 | 263 | 2.7× |
  | 16 × 16k | 27 | 945 | **59.1** | – | – | – |
  | 32 × 8k | 13 | 923 | **57.7** | 924 | 506 | 1.00× |
  | 64 × 4k | 5 | 895 | **55.9** | 951 | – | 0.94× |
  | 128 × 2k | 5 | 904 | **56.5** | – | – | – |

  Error 2.7e-4–2.8e-4 vs float64 everywhere.
- **Conclusions:**
  - Track 2's bandwidth target (≥ 50% at batch ≥ 8) is met: 56–59%.
  - Our kernel is the fastest measured decode at batch ≤ 16 by a wide margin, and on par with vLLM's Triton kernel at 32–64.
  - The two steps that mattered: occupancy (16-key tiles, 16 KB LDS) and more wavefronts per tile (padded rows). Not: a SIMT kernel (negative), larger key tiles (negative).

### 2026-11-05 · Simulated FP8 KV cache on gfx90a (negative result) (`tilelang/gqa_decode.py fp8`)
- **Method:**
  - K/V stored as FP8 e4m3 bit patterns, one scale per tensor (amax/448).
  - Decoded in-kernel through a 256-entry fp16 lookup table in LDS. gfx90a has no FP8 conversion instructions, and TileLang's FP8 types need `fp8-insts`.
  - Same paged layout and tile as the best fp16 kernel (bM 32, 128 threads, ~12 blocks/CU).
- **Compiler bug found:** TileLang 0.1.15's vectorised `uint8` global→LDS copy emits a `make_uint4` call that doesn't compile for gfx90a. Workaround: load the bytes as packed int32 words and unpack with shifts.
- **Results** (Qwen GQA d256):

  | B × L | fp16 ms | fp8-sim ms | fp8 ÷ fp16 time | error vs original fp16 cache | error vs dequantised cache |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 × 131k | 0.729 | 1.368 | 1.88× slower | 3.77% | 0.037% |
  | 8 × 32k | 1.040 | 2.251 | 2.16× slower | 3.79% | 0.035% |
  | 32 × 8k | 1.064 | 2.326 | 2.19× slower | 3.78% | 0.034% |
  | 64 × 4k | 1.057 | 2.328 | 2.20× slower | 3.80% | 0.033% |

- **Conclusions:**
  - On gfx90a, software FP8 decode via LDS lookups costs more than the halved HBM traffic saves.
  - The kernel itself is correct: the 0.034% vs the dequantised cache equals the fp16 kernel's error.
  - The ~3.8% output error is the price of e4m3 with a single scale on Gaussian data; per-page scales would lower it, but weren't measured.
Track 2 FP8 item: measured negative result.

### 2026-11-05 · RCCL collectives on 1, 2 and 4 LUMI-G nodes (`scaling/comm_bench.py`, `scaling/comm.sbatch`)
- **Setup:**
  - LUMI PyTorch container (torch 2.7.1 + ROCm 6.2.4, RCCL with the Slingshot OFI plugin), one task per GCD (`ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES=$SLURM_LOCALID`), LUMI's CPU-affinity masks.
  - `NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME=hsn0-3`, `NCCL_NET_GDR_LEVEL=PHB`; dev-g, jobs 23498771 / 23498780 / 23498932.
  - fp16 per-rank sizes 1 MB–1 GB; time = max over ranks; nccl-tests busbw conventions.
- **Results** (bus bandwidth, GB/s):

  | op, size per rank | 1 node (8 GCD) | 2 nodes (16) | 4 nodes (32) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | all-reduce 8 MB | 48.0 | 39.5 | 13.4 |
  | all-reduce 64 MB | 122.3 | 77.3 | 49.8 |
  | all-reduce 1 GB | 134.1 | 98.3 | 92.6 |
  | all-to-all 8 MB | 42.1 | 16.9 | 12.0 |
  | all-to-all 64 MB | 62.7 | 15.2 | 9.5 |
  | all-to-all 1 GB | 66.8 | 18.5 | 12.1 |

- **Conclusions:**
  - Large all-reduces keep ~70% of single-node bus bandwidth up to 4 nodes (ring/tree over Slingshot), so data-parallel training gradients scale.
  - All-to-all, the expert-parallel MoE dispatch pattern, falls 3.6× when traffic leaves the node and 5.5× at 4 nodes.
  - Expert parallelism should stay inside a node (8 GCDs) on LUMI-G; scale beyond it with data/pipeline parallelism.
  - Small messages (≤ 8 MB) are latency-bound and degrade fastest across nodes.
- Not measured: ROCm 7.2 / vLLM multi-node (LUMI supports ROCm ≤ 7.0); rccl-tests binaries (the torch.distributed numbers include the Python call overhead, which is negligible at ≥ 64 MB).

### 2026-11-05 · Expert-parallel MoE layer on 1 GCD → 4 nodes (`scaling/ep_moe.py`, `scaling/ep.sbatch`)
- **Setup:**
  - MoE layer H 2048, I 512, 256 experts, top-8 (the `moe_fused.py` shape); experts split evenly over W ranks; our TileLang fused-MoE kernels compute locally.
  - Dispatch/combine with `all_to_all_single` (uneven splits); fixed capacity 1.3× the average with a zero dummy expert for padding (fixed shapes, no recompiles). Random uniform routing.
  - Weak scaling: fixed tokens per rank (512, 2048). Times = median of 10 steps, max over ranks.
  - Jobs 23500327 (1 GCD), 23498955 (8), 23498972 (16), 23499049 (32). No rows dropped in any run.
- **Results, 2048 tokens per rank:**

  | GCDs (nodes) | experts/rank | dispatch ms | compute ms | combine ms | step ms | tokens/s total | weak-scaling efficiency |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 (1) | 256 | 0.16 | 4.88 | 0.92 | 6.07 | 0.34 M | – |
  | 8 (1) | 32 | 1.38 | 2.79 | 1.95 | 6.10 | 2.69 M | 99% |
  | 16 (2) | 16 | 3.94 | 2.59 | 4.50 | 11.05 | 2.96 M | 55% |
  | 32 (4) | 8 | 7.20 | 2.55 | 6.82 | 16.07 | 4.08 M | 38% |

- **Results, 512 tokens per rank:** 0.16 M → 2.03 M (8 GCDs, 12.6× because 256 local experts with ~16 rows each run inefficiently on one GCD) → 2.56 M (16) → 3.46 M (32) tokens/s.
- **Conclusions:**
  - Inside one node, expert parallelism is free: the all-to-all costs about what fewer local experts save, so 8 GCDs give 7.9× the tokens/s of one.
  - Across nodes, dispatch + combine grow from 3.3 ms to 8.4 ms (2 nodes) and 14.0 ms (4 nodes) and take 76–87% of the step; this matches the all-to-all busbw drop (67 → 18.5 → 12.1 GB/s).
  - Recommendation for LUMI-G: EP ≤ 8 (one node); scale further with data or pipeline parallelism. Overlapping the all-to-all with compute (chunked dispatch) is the remaining lever, not measured.
  - Track 5 done criterion (RCCL + MoE scaling on 8 GCDs and 2–4 nodes): met.

### 2026-11-05 · Small-M GEMM, split-K rerun with a plain fp16 S=1 path (`gemm_prefetch.py splitk`)
- **Change:** with S = 1 the sweep now uses the plain fp16 tile kernel (no fp32 zero, atomics or cast). One GCD, dev-g.
- **Best per shape vs hipBLASLt** (rel. error 2.1e-4 vs fp32 in all cases):

  | M × N × K | best | config |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 × 5120 × 5120 | 0.94× | S 4, bN 64, bK 128 |
  | 16 × 17408 × 5120 | 0.80× | S 4, bN 64, bK 128 |
  | 32 × 5120 × 5120 | 0.85× | S 4, bN 64, bK 64 |
  | 64 × 5120 × 5120 | 0.82× | S 4, bN 64, bK 64 |
  | 64 × 17408 × 5120 | 0.93× | S 1 (plain), bN 64, bK 64 |
  | 64 × 5120 × 17408 | 1.08× | S 4, bN 64, bK 64 |

- **Conclusion:** all six decode shapes are ≥ 0.80× hipBLASLt (0.80–1.08×). Track 4 small-M criterion met. The 16 × 17408
  shape is on the line; the wide-N shape needs no split because N/64 = 272 tiles already fill 110 CUs.

### 2026-11-05 · Fused MoE round 6: is the indexed row gather the bottleneck? (`moe_fused.py`, mode `v2`)
- **Change:** `moe_gate_up_v2` resolves a block's bM source rows once into LDS (sentinel → a zero row of a padded X), so the per-K-step load has no branch. Also timed the contiguous-X kernel alone (`gate_up_contig_ms`), excluding the gather that prepares its input, as an upper bound for any gather improvement.
- **Results, T = 4,096** (error 3.1e-4 vs fp32 everywhere):

  | bM×bN×bK | mode | total ms | gate/up ms | down ms |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 64×64×64 | indexed (old) | 6.22 | 2.94 | 2.64 |
  | 64×64×64 | contiguous X, kernel alone | – | 3.09 | – |
  | 64×64×64 | v2 | 6.17 | 2.89 | 2.64 |
  | 64×128×64 | v2 | 7.16 | 3.95 | 2.61 |
  | 128×64×64 | v2 | 8.07 | 4.64 | 2.83 |
  | 64×64×32 | v2 | 7.59 | 3.92 | 2.87 |

- **T = 1,024:** old 2.75 ms, v2 2.74 ms; other tiles slower.
- **Conclusions:** the gather is not the bottleneck (the hypothesis from round 4 is refuted; v2 gains < 2%). The time is in the GEMM loop and in `down`, whose input is already contiguous. vLLM remains ahead at large T (3.85 ms at 4,096).

### 2026-11-05 · Dense GEMM: register staging with an explicit thread map (`gemm_prefetch.py`, variant `regstage`), negative
- **Idea:** load tile k+1 global → per-thread registers before the MFMAs of tile k, store to LDS after them, with thread t owning 16-byte vectors `v*threads + t`, to avoid the fragment layouts that made `regpref` slow. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** fragment layouts were not the cause; scratch memory was, here as well (see line 141).
- **Results vs hipBLASLt** (error 2.1e-4; base = same tiles, no staging):

  | shape | hipBLASLt TF | base 128×128×64 | regstage 128×128×64 | base 256×128×32 | regstage 256×128×32 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4096³ | 108.0 | 99.0 (0.92×) | 26.3 (0.24×) | 52.3 | 48.3 |
  | 8192³ | 113.5 | 83.2 (0.73×) | 24.8 (0.22×) | 53.4 | 42.9 |
  | 4096×17408×5120 | 113.4 | 89.3 (0.79×) | 25.7 (0.23×) | 51.4 | 41.2 |
  | 32768×1024×2048 | 99.3 | 103.1 (1.04×) | 27.0 (0.27×) | 52.5 | 46.5 |

- **Reading:** staging as written costs 3.7× at 128 rows and is independent of bK (~27 TF at bK 32 and 64), which suggests a code-generation limit rather than memory overlap. Cause not established yet; generated code to be inspected. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** inspected since. The staging arrays stay in scratch, so no overlap happened. `uint4` scalars give ScratchSize 0 and 25.21 to 102.56 TF at 128×128×64, 4096³, in one unrepeated run. Why bK does not matter is still unexplained (`writeup/vendor-kernel-analysis.md` section 5.2).
- **Correction to an earlier statement:** that CK overlaps loads through register staging is a hypothesis; CK's source has not been checked yet. **Correction (5 Nov 2026):** checked since, in source and binary. It is half true: V uses register staging, K uses direct global→LDS loads.

- **Setup:** C++ harness, cached TileLang C patched with regular expressions, all variants in one translation unit. 5 warm-up, 40 timed, one run per configuration. dev-g, 24 s, one GCD. Raw `evidence/gap-analysis/lumi-analysis/mb/run1.out`. Checksums identical per shape. The MoE rows use synthetic inputs and have no correctness check.

  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4096³ | 101.16 | 52.59 | 84.10 | 104.61 | 84.38 | 107.04 |
  | 8192³ | 83.09 | 53.41 | 98.12 | 90.37 | not run | 105.71 |
  | 32768×1024×2048 | 105.23 | 51.88 | 97.54 | not run | not run | not run |

  Scratch staging (`r12864`) 25.21 TF, the same tile with scalars (`rr12864`) 102.56 TF, both at 4096³.

### 2026-11-05 · E1: dense GEMM, dispatch geometry, clock, one-kernel compiles, same-job hipBLASLt
- **Setup:** dev-g, one GCD, ROCm 6.2.4 container, private TileLang cache. Jobs 23501633, 23501808, 23501904, 23502054, 23502065; 665 s = 0.185 GPU-hours. Bare C++ harness, interleaved rounds. Raw `evidence/E1/`. Output checksums are identical across all non-32×32 variants per shape.
- **Deviation from the plan:** the planned tile pairs (128×128×32 at 1320 blocks, rr25632w2 at 880) were replaced by equal-footprint pairs, to remove a footprint confound. So b12832 was not run at an exact multiple, and the plan's "about 113 TF at P=3" remains untested.
- **MFMA micro, `mfma_only`, P=3, median of 5 rounds, spread ≤ 1.3% (nk 64), ≤ 0.55% (nk 640):**

  | Blocks B | ceil(B/110) | TF, nk 64 | TF, nk 640 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 880 | 8 | 155.28 | 156.50 |
  | 1024 | 10 | 146.19 | 147.25 |
  | 1100 | 10 | 155.32 | 156.53 |
  | 1320 | 12 | 155.49 | 156.56 |
  | 1321 | 13 | 145.31 | |

- **Direct hardware reads** (`s_memtime`, `s_memrealtime`, HW_ID per wave; micro only): 110 CUs, blocks per CU at floor or ceil of B/110, peak blocks on a CU equal to P, 32.04 ticks per 16×16×16 MFMA and 64.08 per 32×32×8.
- **Clock, MHz, `mfma_tr` by active CUs:** 11: 1606; 28: 1560; 55: 1497; 83: 1432; 110: 1381. Nop loop 1625, VALU loop 1523. 32×32×8 at 110 CUs: 1262–1263 on one node, 1273–1280 on another. Real GEMM kernels (stamped builds, one node, 3 rounds): 1219–1420 MHz. These sweeps are short bursts in a fixed order; only the 110-CU level has a sustained run behind it.
- **`rocm-smi`** during the sustain: 1650 MHz in 23 of 24 samples, while traces in that job read 1391–1395 MHz and the sustained TF implies about 1390 MHz. It also reports "Performance Level: manual", which we did not investigate. Package power plateaus at 295 W (16×16×16) and 303 W (32×32×8): the lower-clock kernel draws more, so a single fixed package cap does not simply fit. A power limit as the cause is a hypothesis; no limit register was read.
- **Compile resources** (V, A, Total, Occupancy), alone vs all variants in one file: b25632w2 166,0,166,3 vs 192,0,192,2; rr25632w2 196,0,196,2 vs 212,0,212,2. Speed alone vs combined: b25632w2 +5.8, +8.0, +4.4, +4.1% with non-overlapping ranges; rr25632w2 within 1%. Cause not traced; one binary pair per shape; hand-compiled C, not the TileLang path.
- **Direct hipBLASLt from C++, same process and data, TF** (job 23501904, fifth row and 16384³ job 23502054; `hbm_bf_table.txt`):

  | Shape | hipBLASLt | b12864 | b25632w2 | rr12864w3 | rr25632w2 | best ratio |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4096³ | 111.65 | 102.71 | 90.15 | 106.05 | 108.77 | 0.974 |
  | 8192³ | 118.06 | 83.11 | 106.37 | 88.56 | 104.99 | 0.901 |
  | 32768×1024×2048 | 102.76 | 107.07 | 102.99 | 108.72 | 107.51 | 1.058 |
  | 4096×17408×5120 | 117.52 | 89.03 | 103.19 | 88.19 | 109.65 | 0.933 |
  | 8192³ (repeat) | 118.75 | 83.12 | 107.15 | 89.40 | 106.40 | 0.902 |
  | 16384³ | 121.72 | 62.99 | 94.23 | 79.38 | 87.68 | 0.774 |

  Numeric agreement with hipBLASLt: relative max difference 3.9e-4 to 5.9e-4. Limits: fixed-exponent synthetic data; the rounds are one process and one allocation with hipBLASLt first in each; only 8192³ was repeated in a second process; 16384³ has 3 rounds of 4 iterations.
- **hipBLASLt through torch** (job 23501808, same job and node, other process, other data and timer): 105.32 / 110.31 / 96.64 / 109.48 TF, rr25632w2 alone 106.61 / 104.25 / 105.37 / 109.38, ratios 1.012 / 0.945 / 1.090 / 0.999; rr12864w3 1.100 at the 32768 shape.
- **Torch vs direct is not settled.** The earlier near-1.0 ratios mixed a bare harness with torch timing, so they are not like for like. The gap scales with problem size (0.074 ms at 4k, 0.39 at 8k, 3.54 at 16k), so it is not a fixed per-call cost. Most comparisons cross nodes, and data (randn vs fixed exponent) is not split out. Direct hipBLASLt on randn data was not run, so the real size of the correction is unknown.
- **hipBLASLt kernels at runtime** (`out/hblog_23501904.txt`, `out/hbgrid_23502065.txt`; first capture): MT256×128×64 at 4096³ and 8192³, MT128×128×64 at the 32768 shape, MT128×256×64 at the 17408 shape, all MI32×32. Blocks 512 / 2048 / 2048 / 2176, group_seg 65536 / 65536 / 36864 / 55296, so two blocks per CU would exceed 64 KiB in all cases (P=1 inferred from this, not read). The 16384³ kernel was not logged.
- **Equal footprint, exact vs near multiple of 110, K = 4096, one job:**

  | Kernel | Gain, two pairs |
  |---|---|
  | rr12864w3 | +7.16, +7.04% |
  | b12864 | +6.71, +4.73% |
  | rr25632w2 | +4.47, +3.63% |
  | b25632w2 | +0.06, −1.32% (ranges overlap) |

  The MFMA-only rule predicted +9.6% and +19.6%.
- **bf16 vs fp16:** the README compared different tiles. Same tile 128×128×64 through the wrapper: 83.13 vs 81.35 TF at 8192³, 62.19 vs 62.48 at 16384³. At 256×128×32 fp16 has Occupancy 1 and bf16 Occupancy 2: 53.14 vs 98.60 TF. fp16 with `w2` on the bare harness beats bf16 by about 6% at 8192³ and loses by about 4% at 16384³ (94.23 vs 97.67, swizzle 8), across harnesses. bf16 with the attribute is untested. hipBLASLt bf16 is +5.0 and +5.1% over fp16 through torch.
- **32×32×8:** same flops per tick, lower clock: 143.09 vs 156.53 TF in the micro. Timing-only swaps in real kernels −5.1 to +0.5%, numerics invalid.
- **Conclusions:**
  - 146 TF is block-count rounding, not a ceiling. Observed sustained 156.6 TF on this node's clock settings; whether 191.5 is reachable under other settings is unverified.
  - The section 5.5 rounds fit is refuted as written, by about 18% at P=3 and 11–12% at P=2 at equal scale; the other 18% of its miss at B=1320 is the 1.7 GHz peak.
  - ceil(B/110) models the MFMA micro with worst error 2.4% for B ≥ 880. P has about a 1% effect (P=1 lower), grid shape none. It is not a law for GEMM: real kernels leave floor/ceil at large B (rr25632w2 at 8192³ runs 17–20 blocks per CU against 18.6), and it over-predicts gains. Report B and B/110 in tables; do not normalize real TF with it.
  - The 98–107 TF results stand on one-kernel compiles. Compiling alone helps b25632w2. The fork fixes C1 and C2 have support; C2's value depends on shape (rr25632w2 over b25632w2: +20.7% at 4096³, +6.3% at 17408, −1.3% at 8192³, −7.0% at 16384³).
  - Drop 32×32×8 as a raw-throughput fork path, per its gate. Whether it helps in a correct 256×128×64 kernel is unknown: hipBLASLt, the fastest code here, uses MI32×32 on every shape logged.
  - bf16 is not faster at the same tile; 100.1 TF is real (100.61 repeated).
  - 128-row kernels at the 17408 shape are bimodal, about 89 vs 94 TF: low in the first two rounds of job 23501808 (each round a separate process) and in all 5 rounds of the single process in job 23501904. So the mode goes with the process or early job; placement is an untested hypothesis.

### 2026-11-05 · K1: direct global→LDS load on MI250X, against real register staging
- **Setup:** small-g, one GCD, ROCm 6.2.4, one code object per GEMM kernel, 5 warm-up + 40 timed, 3 rotated rounds. Jobs 23501703, 23501734 (racy first kernels, timings superseded), 23501841; 377 s = 0.105 GPU-hours. Raw `evidence/K1/raw/`.
- **Works on this GPU:** over 3e9 dwords checked per hazard mode. LDS address = M0 + lane×4, M0 in bytes, rounded down to a dword; lane equals `threadIdx & 63`. M0 ≥ 0x10000 wraps to the low offset (bit 16 tested, the rest per the ISA).
- **Errors are silent:** out-of-range lanes are dropped, EXEC still reads all ones, TRAPSTS unchanged. Beyond the V# size, zeros are written with no fault. An unmapped VA is a real fault that kills the process.
- **`vmcnt(N)`** is exact and in issue order: about 1e9 lane checks at `vmcnt(8)`, 0 misses in chunk 1, with data shown in flight. A first test was void because volatile LDS reads make the compiler insert `vmcnt(0)`.
- **Hazards:** VALU writes SGPR then VMEM: 36.6–90.6% wrong data with up to 3 wait states, 0 with 4 or 5 (ISA printed page 24 asks for 5). M0 wait state: no failure either way, with no positive control. TileLang's `cp_async_gs<4>` template is exposed to the first (`readfirstlane` before the asm, `pre_nop` unused) and showed no errors in the tested compile, for reasons not traced.
- **GEMM, TF, mean of 3 rounds** (hipBLASLt through torch, same allocation):

  | Kernel | 4096³ | 8192³ |
  |---|---|---|
  | hipBLASLt | 109.24 | 115.01 |
  | rr12864w3, generator kernel, staging | 104.33 | 89.11 |
  | g5_64 direct, no overlap | 94.21 | 94.24 |
  | g1_64 hand staging | 91.39 | 91.93 |
  | g4_32w3 / g2_32 direct, overlap, best | 87.07 | 75.31 |
  | g0_32 synchronous | 87.00 | 77.16 |
  | g3_32 direct, no overlap | | 79.29 |
  | g1_32 hand staging (dyn 32768) | 85.95 | 71.67 |

- **Overlap at 1 block/CU, 4096³:** saved share of the smaller of load and math time: staged 0.78, direct 0.40–0.52, synchronous 0.14.
- **Same tile, overlap on:** best direct vs staging at 1 block/CU: staging +23–26% at bK 32, +19–21% at bK 64. At 2 blocks/CU a tie at 4096³; at 8192³ direct is +1.3% best-dyn to best-dyn, and neither overlap kernel beats synchronous g0_32.
- **Without overlap, direct is not worse:** hand-written direct beats hand-written staging at 2 blocks/CU: +3.1% and +2.5% at bK 64, +10.6% at bK 32 at 8192³ (79.29 vs 71.67, a weak staged point). Direct loses only to the generator kernel, and only at 4096³. Hand vs generator differs by 14% at 4096³, more than the class gap, so the 4096³ ordering cannot be read as load path alone.
- **Structural limit:** direct overlap needs a spare LDS slot. 128×128×64 then needs 65536 B and falls to 1 block/CU at 52.8 TF, while staging runs that tile at 2 blocks/CU.
- **Race found:** first kernels overwrote a slot another wave was still reading. Wrong checksums exposed one of the two kernels. Rule: 2 slots need a barrier before the issue; only 3 slots allow CK's single barrier.
- **Coverage of the error check:** C is read back after the last of 45 launches, so 44 launches per row are unchecked. For a family whose bug was intermittent, the barrier fix rests on code reading plus that sample.
- **Also:** dyn = 65536 dispatches and the checked launches are correct, so "exactly 64 KB does not launch" is wrong at this value. Clock 1.631–1.632 GHz with one block, 1.386 GHz in 24–73 ms all-CU MFMA bursts. Source reading of the gates: `copy.h:111-130` keeps N == 16 and N == 8 synchronous, so opening the two target gates alone would not make an fp16 16-byte copy asynchronous (not compiled through the pipeline).
- **Conclusions:**
  - "Not yet shown running by us" in the earlier correction notes is now closed: it runs.
  - K1 gives no evidence that C4 as tested would close the hipBLASLt gap, so C4 should not lead for GEMM. That is a judgment made after the data; no threshold was fixed in advance. Whether C1 + C2 is enough is not shown either: at 8192³ the 128-row kernels here stay 18–20% short.
  - Not tested: CK's real mix (one input direct, one staged), its 272 B stride, 32×32×8, 256×128 tiles with direct loads. So "does our path match CK's" stays open.
  - C8 remains as written in the plan: only if C2 is stable and C4 loses. K1 moves it toward triggering once C2 is shown stable.

### 2026-11-05 · E2: MoE on identical inputs, vLLM's stage split, same-stack comparison
- **Setup:** small-g, one GCD. H 2048, I 512, E 256, top-8, from one CPU-generated data file loaded by both stacks. Routing is the script's uniform softmax top-8, not a model's; the shape is still recalled. Tile 64×64×64 gate/up, 64×128×64 down, 256 threads, k_pack 2. Events per stage, 5 warm-up, 30 or 50 repeats, 3 cycles, mean of per-cycle medians. Jobs 23501695, 23501806 (failed compile check, fixed), 23501828, 23502058, 23502121, 23502352, 23502474; 44:45 = 0.75 GPU-hours. Raw `evidence/E2/raw/`. TileLang 0.1.15 also ran inside the vLLM image with its hipcc (ROCm 7.2.3, clang 22), so a same-stack column exists for the first time.
- **vLLM stages for the first time, T = 4096:** align 0.038, k1 2.078, `silu_and_mul` 0.095, k2 1.501, `moe_sum` 0.153, total 3.868 ms with events, 3.841 plain. T = 1024: 0.024 / 1.324 / 0.037 / 0.840 / 0.046, total 2.270, 2.240 plain.
- **Best arm `F5_mg_dump`:** gate/up v2 X path, padding rows unwritten, safe-memory pass off, swapped grid; down with Sid and Wt once per thread, dump row, swapped grid; two-kernel align; top-k sum kernel. Error 3.134e-4 (T = 1024) and 3.136e-4 (T = 4096) vs fp32; vLLM 5.378e-4 and 5.382e-4. Ours has 5 events, vLLM plain 2. It reads padding from a zero row of an X buffer built once outside the timed loop.

  | T | Job | vLLM plain ms | F5, ROCm 6.2.4 / clang 18 | F5, vLLM image | F3 with X copy timed, 6.2.4 / image |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4096 | 23502121 | 3.698 | 3.879, 1.049x | 3.733, 1.009x | 3.930, 1.063x / 3.790, 1.025x |
  | 4096 | 23502352 | 3.837 | 3.918, 1.021x | 3.796, 0.989x | 3.985, 1.039x / 3.865, 1.007x |
  | 1024 | 23502121 | 2.218 | 2.012, 0.907x | 2.019, 0.911x | |
  | 1024 | 23502352 | 2.238 | 2.027, 0.906x | 2.035, 0.909x | |

  Ratios are our time over vLLM's. On an events basis both ways: 3.879/3.734 = 1.039, 3.733/3.734 = 1.000, 3.918/3.875 = 1.011, 3.796/3.875 = 0.980. F5 with the copy timed was not run. Against vLLM at bM 64 at T = 1024: 2.012/2.187 = 0.920, so about 8% rather than 9%; half the rows at that size are padding under uniform routing, so that lead is specific to this routing.
- **Saving within one job, 6.2.4 stack:** 6.060 → 3.879 ms (2.181 ms, 36.0%, 23502121), 6.284 → 3.918 ms (2.366 ms, 37.7%, 23502352).
- **Ablation, T = 4096, job 23502058, ms:**

  | Arm | 6.2.4 total | stages (align/k1/k2/sum) | Image total |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | base | 6.283 | .140/3.084/2.541/.511 | 5.930 |
  | safe-memory off, gate/up | 6.036 | .138/2.844/2.543/.509 | 5.723 |
  | safe-memory off, down | 6.374 | .139/3.082/2.641/.510 | 5.495 |
  | down hoist | 6.069 | .139/3.086/2.332/.511 | 5.733 |
  | all planned together | 5.927 | .138/2.851/2.425/.512 | 5.470 |
  | + v2 | 5.692 | .138/2.613/2.427/.514 | 5.252 |
  | + sum kernel | 5.329 | .137/2.617/2.426/.151 | 5.247 |
  | + grid swap, both | 4.270 | .138/2.125/1.855/.152 | 4.112 |
  | swap + dump row | 4.091 | .139/2.162/1.637/.154 | 4.015 |
  | swap, hoist, two-kernel align | 4.231 | .033/2.163/1.882/.153 | 4.136 |

- **Levers:** grid swap saves 0.94–1.06 ms on 6.2.4 and 0.97–1.14 ms in the image, but only measured last, on top of the other fixes; swap on base was not run, and it changes the grid tuple and b0/b1 mapping in two kernels. Other levers were mostly measured first-in, so the ranking depends on order. v2 + safe-memory off on gate/up 0.467 ms. Top-k sum kernel 0.360 ms on torch 2.7.1 only; torch 2.12's own sum is already 0.157 ms. Dump row 0.22 ms (branches also fall, 21 to 5). Two-kernel align 0.107 ms. Down hoist 0.209 ms alone. Safe-memory off on gate/up 0.240 ms.
- **Refuted:**
  - Empty launches: 5.326 vs 5.329 ms.
  - Per-call `torch.empty`: 5.337 ms.
  - `do_bench` L2 flush: 6.281 vs 6.283 ms, vLLM 3.843 vs 3.841. This shows the flush does not change results, not that it evicts L2.
  - Padding and bytes as the main explanation. Direct evidence: our mask arms give only 0.02–0.07 ms at T = 4096, and the swap gains about 1 ms with bytes unchanged. vLLM's bM 64 arm (3.890 vs 3.734, about 4%) does not isolate padding, because block count changes with it.
  - The 0.40 and 0.82 ms synthetic-input figures, as forecasts for real inputs.
  - The plan's 5.2 ms forecast was not met by the planned runs alone (5.927 and 5.470 ms). The gain came from levers the plan did not rate.
- **Hoist rule:** it fired (0.209 ms < 0.3) and launches are excluded, but no measurement shows stores dominate down. The largest down saving is dispatch order (0.571 ms), which is neither stores, launches nor Sid/Wt loads.
- **Compiler flips:** safe-memory off on down: clang 18 2.541 → 2.641 ms, clang 22 2.576 → 2.138. Hoist with safe-memory off: clang 18 2.641 → 2.425, clang 22 2.137 → 2.325. Never carry a down variant across compilers. Compiler and ROCm/torch are confounded: clang 18 objects never ran under ROCm 7.2.3, nor the reverse.
- **Clang 22 vs forced unroll:** default clang 18 keeps 32 MFMA with no overlap in down, clang 22 unrolls to 256 MFMA with loads interleaved. Forced clang 18 (`HIPCC_COMPILE_FLAGS_APPEND`) reaches the same shape, yet same-job k2 is 1.605 vs 1.532 ms, both at 256 MFMA, so overlap alone does not explain k2. Threshold 2000 recovers nothing in that job. A "30% recovered" claim in the results file mixed jobs and is withdrawn: same-job k2 at threshold 20000 is 1.522, 1.593, 1.592 by cycle, about 11% on the two stable cycles. At T = 1024 forced unroll hurts: total 2.085 vs 2.012–2.027 ms.
- **Open result, not a one-off:** F5 at threshold 20000 reproduces in 3 of 3 processes: k1 2.025, 2.048, 2.048 ms against F3 and F1 near 2.72–2.74 in the same cycles; T = 1024 k1 1.261, 1.249, 1.261 vs 1.452, 1.438, 1.453. Total 3.802 vs same-job vLLM 3.809, 0.998x, the best number on this stack. It conflicts with "unrolled gate/up is slower" and has no explanation yet.
- **`compile_flags`:** flags had no effect on the HIP path (32 MFMA unchanged). The results file blames `libgen.py`; that is wrong, since `libgen.py:186` appends them after the HIP branch. `TL_DEVICE_COMPILE_FLAGS` is read by the CUDA and Ascend backends but nowhere in `tilelang/rocm`; call path not traced.
- **Limits:** arm order is fixed, with `--rounds` 1, so arm and time-in-process are confounded. Job effects hit kernels unequally: −1.2% to −5.4% on the same node between jobs 73 s apart, and F5/vLLM moved 2.0 points between two jobs, more than the same-stack difference. Headline ratios effectively rest on two jobs. No threshold for "tie" was fixed in advance. No counters or clock under load, so "the swap helps through L2 reuse" is a hypothesis.
- **Conclusions:** the 6.2 vs 3.85 ms gap closes for the kernels timed, with source and flag changes and no fork build, on this dataset and routing. On vLLM's stack F5 is within 1% of vLLM in both jobs. On our stack it is 1.021–1.049x, nearly all in k2. At T = 1024 we lead by about 8–9%, on uniform routing. None of this is in `moe_fused.py` yet.

### 2026-11-06 · E3: prefill attribution, same allocation as CK
- **Setup:** small-g, one GCD, 12 jobs (23501691, 23501830, 23502084, 23502161, 23502322, 23502441, 23502624, 23502772, 23503014, 23503278, 23503338, 23503389), 64:30 = 1.075 GPU-hours. Raw `evidence/E3/raw/`. 10 warm-up, 100 calls, 3–5 interleaved rounds. TileLang on ROCm 6.2.4 / torch 2.7.1 / clang 18, CK on ROCm 7.2.3 / torch 2.12 / clang 22, so "same job" means same GPU and node, not same stack. The decision rule was written before the first job.
- **Decision, d256 8k, P32 bN16, 768 threads, job 23501830, 5 rounds:** base 11.245, compute-only `comp_b` 10.389, MFMA + barriers `mfma_b` 7.820, CK 9.244 ms. D_L 0.856, D_I 2.569, L_share 0.25, R_w 0.82, R_b 1.117, M1 0.43. **Verdict under the rule: no single driver.** L_share meets the instruction side (≤ 0.40), R_I 1.117 misses 1.15. Second job 23502322 on another node: L_share 0.337, M1 0.565. mfma_b has one barrier fewer (disclosed) and Occupancy 4 against 3 (not in the original report).
- **Load stalls are not the main driver at bN16 768 threads** (L_share 0.25–0.34, d128 0.22; M1 0.43–0.57, d128 0.18). At larger bN loads are not excluded: bN48 L_share 0.53, M1 0.85–1.02 across jobs, contaminated by ScratchSize 16 in base. A cross-job spill-free estimate gives 0.31 at bN48, an indication only.
- **Best results passing the error check, same job, TF:**

  | Case | Base | TileLang only | With C patches | CK | Ratios to CK |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | d256 8k (23503278) | 73.80 | 83.34, bN48, edge + reverse | 85.62, bN48, edge + reverse + reciprocal, patches A, B, D | 89.70 | 0.823, 0.929, 0.955 |
  | d128 4k (23503338) | 62.47 | 72.44, bN96, edge + reverse + reciprocal | 76.09, same + A, B, D | 93.23 | 0.670, 0.777, 0.816 |
  | MHA d128 4k (23503389) | 49.49 at P192 bN32 | 69.29, P128 bN32, 512 threads | 68.35, P192 bN96, reciprocal + patches | 82.05 | 0.603, 0.844, 0.833 |

  Errors 2.59e-4, 2.57e-4, 2.84e-4; CK 4.07e-4 to 4.51e-4. **Label correction:** the results call the patched rows "KXABD", but patches K and X were skipped by their regexes on 34 of 48 configs, including every best tile, so those rows are A, B, D. The 85.62 kernel still has ScratchSize 16. Patches are C edits on generated source, not TileLang features: A removes 2 of 4 barriers around a reduce that never touches LDS, B reduces `l_i` once after the K loop, D skips the O rescale when no lane changes.
- **Patch checks:** 30/30 bitwise repeats on one `torch.randn` input, round 1 only. This is not a race-freedom proof: patch A on transposed-V kernels passes at some tiles and fails at others (3.27e-3, 0.22).
- **KX (16-byte lane loads in `T.copy`):** observed gain only at d256 bN16, 73.15 → 76.03, +3.9%. "No change at bN48" in the results is not a measurement, since the patch did not apply there.
- **Reciprocal:** noise where nothing spills. Where registers are at the cap it moves the spill: bN32 fullD 75.12 vs full 56.96 TF (+31.9%, ScratchSize 76 → 28); bN32 fullERD 69.57 vs fullER 49.97 (+39%); bN48 fullERD 75.85 vs fullER 83.34 (−9.0%, ScratchSize 0 → 48). So the bests at bN32 and bN48 are fragile against any source change.
- **Edge mask and reverse dispatch:** at d256 bN16 slower, 68.58 vs 73.33 TF. At bN48 edge is mostly a spill fix (ScratchSize 16 → 0, 75.26 → 83.17 TF). At d128 base tile +1.8% to +4.3% across 2 jobs.
- **Fewer waves (LOG:56):** slower without spill, up to 1.78x (d128 384 threads). At d256 384 threads, spill and the load path are confounded (2.77x full with ScratchSize 132, 1.22x compute-only without), so spill's own share is not measured. One exception: d128 P64 512 threads +7.8%.
- **Compute-only breakdown at d256:** removing reduce_max saves 2.307 ms, exp/sum/cast 1.308, rescale 0.366, mask none. These run on altered data (-inf, NaN, Inf, unbounded accumulation) and may not transfer, so treat them as indications, not costs. Real savings: patches B then D take `comp_b` 10.452 → 9.458 → 9.149 ms (job 23502624).
- **V read:** transposing V saves 1.195 ms compute-only at bN16 (+12.9%, job 23502322). The 105.81 TF "1.18x CK" row removes all loads and applies patches, so it is not a forecast. Correct kernels with V transposed reach only 51.9–58.2 TF. Per-element LDS writes as the cause is a hypothesis. CK's own form (2 row loads, `v_perm_b32`, `ds_write2_b32`) was not tried.
- **MFMA-only time** is 7.99 − 0.573 = 7.42 ms, 1.72x the 4.31 ms nominal at 191.5 TF. Single GEMMs are 3.415 and 3.749 ms, each 1.6–1.7x of half-nominal, so the excess is in both and mostly additive. Using E1's 156.6 TF instead: 8.246e11 / 156.6e12 = 5.27 ms, 7.42/5.27 = 1.41x. That clock was not measured in this kernel [hypothesis], and the kernel also keeps dead branches and LDS reads.
- **Stack reference:** hipBLASLt-routed GEMM 8192³ is equal across stacks within 0.3% (110.98 vs 110.92). SDPA is 1.10–1.44x faster on the newer stack (d256 62.86 vs 69.00, MHA 47.32 vs 67.96), cause unknown. The CK ratios above are therefore cross-stack, and no same-stack run exists. d256 8k TileLang-only against same-job newer-stack SDPA: 83.34/68.82 = 1.21x.
- **MHA:** 0.51x in LOG:170 becomes 0.84x by moving from the upstream kernel and tile to the GQA form at G=1 across jobs. Within E3 the tile alone gives 0.603–0.844x.
- **Not settled:** bN64 at d256 was skipped as illegal, but no compile or run tested it, and K1 shows 65536 B dispatches. Barrier cost of about 194 cycles rests on an assumed 1.65 GHz and on dynamic counts that cannot be rechecked because generated kernels were deleted. Warm-up is 10 calls, about 10–15 ms at d128 and MHA, with an idle clock of 800 MHz and no measured ramp, so those TF may carry a bias. `rocm-smi` counts 78 samples at 1650 MHz and 116 at 800; clock during benchmark windows is unmeasured.
- **Conclusions:** direct-to-LDS should not lead prefill. Tile and spill choices alone gain +12.9%, +16.0% and +40%. Nothing passing the error check reached CK on this stack. The remaining gap is unattributed, and MFMA-only time is the largest unexplained term.

### 2026-11-06 · Kernel design review, redone independently (offline, no GPU)
- **Setup:** first-principles arithmetic written before reading the analysis's conclusions, then compared with it. Offline compiles of cached kernels on LUMI, `analysis/rv2/`: `39ce44d6dd29` (128 threads), `a34c3ecd7643` (256), `b7f5b9501694` (64), `e4cdbab35e43` and `4070f5868293` (d128), plus `kr_scan.json` over 883 cached kernels. Not independently reviewed. Resources and load counts in the tables below were rechecked against `rv2/*.ru.json` and `*.s`; the rest is as the review wrote it. It predates E1–E3, so its 146 TF and 1.298 GHz figures are superseded (E1 entry), and every timing below is pre-E2.
- **Compiled limits** (blocks per CU inferred as min(LDS limit, wave slots / waves per block)):

  | Case | VGPR+AGPR | Occupancy | Waves per block | LDS limit | Blocks per CU |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Prefill d256 | 158+0 | 3 | 12 | 4 | 1, registers |
  | Prefill d128 bN 32 | 120+0 | 4 | 12 | 4 | 1, 4 slots idle |
  | MoE gate/up | 104+64 | 3 | 4 | 2 | 2, LDS |
  | Decode 64 threads | 171+64 | 2 | 1 | 4 | 4, LDS |
  | Decode 128 threads | 170+64 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 4, tie |
  | Decode 256 threads | 168+64 | 2 | 4 | 4 | 2, registers |
  | GEMM 128×128×64 | 83+64 | 3 | 4 | 2 | 2, LDS |

- **Decode wave-level counts** (per wave, compiled): 64 threads 8 load rounds (16 `dwordx4`), 475 VALU; 128 threads 4 rounds (8), 359 VALU; 256 threads 2 rounds (4), 301 VALU; all 32 MFMA. Generated source also has `__syncthreads()` between K and V. A one-parameter model, step = rounds × L + waves per SIMD × (1024 + 4·VALU + 60)/clock, with L = 1.23 µs at 1.7 GHz gives 622 / 914 / 700 GB/s against measured 580–586 / 923 / 696. This fits three points with L free and an assumed clock, ignores combine and launch, and uses kernels with other B and L values. **Hypothesis, not tested.**
- **Decode bound:** 229376 tokens × 4096 B = 0.9395 GB, floor 0.587 ms at 1.6 TB/s, measured 1.0185 ms at 32×8k, 1.73x. Padding executes 5.33× the useful flops, and wave 1 does only zero math.
- **Prefill d128 bN curve** (non-MFMA CU-µs per MF, computed with the now-superseded 146 TF, shape only): bN 16 1.556, bN 32 0.980, bN 64 1.049.
- **Its forecasts against what was then measured:**

  | Review forecast | Measured | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | GEMM 256×128×32 with occupancy and staging, 83 → 106 TF | Holds on one-kernel compiles: 83.11 → 106.37, b25632w2 (attribute only) | E1 |
  | Down hoist about 0.42 ms | 0.209 ms alone | E2 |
  | Gate/up guard 0.40 ms | Safe-memory off 0.240 ms | E2 |
  | Top-k up to 0.415 ms | 0.360 ms, torch 2.7.1 only | E2 |
  | MoE combined about 5.34 ms | Planned fixes 5.927 ms; best 3.879 ms through a grid swap it did not rank | E2 |
  | Final divide about 1.3%, no lever | Noise where nothing spills | E3 |
  | V's `ds_read_u16`, net effect unknown | V layout +12.9% compute-only; correct kernels not there yet | E3 |
  | Prefill load rounds at most 12–29% | Not tested as such; L_share 0.25–0.34 | E3 |

- **Corrections it made to the earlier analysis, kept:** decode has compiled counts (168–171 VGPR + 64 AGPR, Occupancy 2), where the analysis said estimates were never compiled. "No known gap" for decode is wrong given a 1.73x gap to the HBM floor. The old report's "about 105 registers per thread, 2.5× margin" is refuted.
- **Conclusion:** decode has the largest arithmetic gap with no experiment yet. The proposed test patches the cached 128-thread kernel to batch K+V loads and drop the barrier, separately stops math on wave 1, and times `k1` and `k2` apart. Near 1.6 TB/s supports the model; no movement refutes it.

### 2026-11-06 · N4, K2, N7: GEMM against hipBLASLt (`evidence/N4/RESULTS.md`)
Ratios: ours over hipBLASLt (hb), throughput, higher is better, same process, rotated arms, randn unless stated. Review corrections are applied here over the first reports.
- **Jobs:** round 1: 23503694, 23503812, 23503870, 23503877, 23503951, 23504003, 23504062, 23504069, 23504136, 23504175, 23504321, 23504325, 23504375, 23504377, 23504420, 23504472, 23504473, 23504475, 23504490, 23504491, 23504582, 23504662, 23504664, 23504679, 23504715 (8586 s = 2.385 GPU-hours). Review: 23504765, 23504955, 2 GPUs each, both hit the time limit (0.544). Round 2: 23505118, 23505119, 23505149, 23505901, 23506098, 23506131, 23506132, 23506192; 23505516 cancelled before start (2.00, budget about 1). Total 17742 GPU-seconds = 4.93 GPU-hours (sacct). Failed or void: 23504321 (script permission), 23504490 (cancelled), 23503877 (out-of-range load, fixed), the 32x32x8 arms of 23504325 (numerically wrong).
- **What landed:** `gemm_prefetch.py` `hand_source`, `gemm_hand`, `hand_tile`, `select_mode`, launched through `T.Kernel(prelude=...)` and `T.call_extern`. The kernel is hand-written inline asm: 256x128x64 or 128x256x64, 16x16x16 MFMA, memory ops spread between MFMAs. `T.CUDASourceCodeKernel` does not work on HIP [observed]. This is not generated by TileLang; porting the schedule to a pass is open.
- **Rule:** hand 256x128 c8 when M%256, N%128, K%64 are 0 and K ≥ 192; 128x256 c8 at exactly 6144³ and 16384³; otherwise `select_tile`; otherwise hipBLASLt.
- **Standing:**

  | 8192³ | 0.837–0.964 | 1.075, 1.072 | 1.029–1.074 | 256x128 c8: 1.043 (1.029–1.046), 1.035 (1.030–1.046) |

  | 16384x4096x4096 | 0.871–0.968 | 1.067, 1.066 | 1.030–1.064 | not run |

- **Reading [computed]:** a real win of about 3–11% at 7 of 9 shapes solo. 6144³ is a tie. 16384³ is thin. Through the rule as landed, one wiring job with no metrics (23506192) reads 6144³ 0.995 and 16384³ 1.008. Script against harness is within 0.5% on 14 of 18 reads. The headline job 23504715 sits near the top of its ranges, so quote ranges.
- **Correctness:** hand output matches hb to 3.5e-4–6.9e-4 relative; FNV identical across arms and jobs per shape. Script `rel_err` against fp32 equals hb's at the precision logged (4 significant digits, 2.07e-4–2.08e-4), checked once before timing.
- **Findings [observed]:**
  - hb at 16384³ is `MT128x256x64 MI32x32 MIWT2_4 MO40`, group_seg 55296.
  - The torch gap is gone: under 0.03 ms with randn on both sides in one job. Fixed-exponent data was the effect.
  - LDS row stride was the largest step: 128 B 106.97 TF, 144 B 117.11, 160 B 119.74. Strides not aligned to 16 B collapse to about 75 TF.
  - Spreading memory ops between MFMAs: +13.8%, across two jobs with hb 110.69 vs 110.50.
  - Block swizzle takes UMC from 48% to 12% at 8192³ only; at 16384³ the same form stays at 54% and loses TF (79.02 to 72.28).
- **Negative [observed]:** LLVM-compiled 256x128x64 with staging 0.72–0.82. Direct loads on 256x128 0.82 at 8192³ (0.995 at 32768). K-start stagger costs 2.5–8%. No generator option clears +1% on 256x128 at 6144³, 12288³, 16384³ (23505149): LDS base offset 0.999–1.001, independent A/B strides −0.5 to −3%, last-load guard +0.4% at K = 2048 and off by default. 128x256 got only c8 and r8 at pad 32, so its stride and panel options are untested. 256x128x32 at 512 threads replaces `w2`: ties at 4 shapes, +4.1% at 16384³.
- **N7, clock and power [observed]:** "Performance Level: manual" is a site setting; DPM stays active, 800 MHz idle, 1650 MHz target. Package cap 500 W. hb runs at 1028–1145 MHz across jobs and conditions at about 91–93% efficiency at 8192³; ours about 1249 MHz, 85%. So hb wins on efficiency, not clock. At the cap the target falls to 1295–1470 MHz and delivered clock falls: hb 1096–1105 to 1028–1095, hand 1222–1251 to 1094–1213. Throttle fields read 0, but their layout was never checked against a header, so they may be mis-decoded. Masking CUs raises clock and lowers TF, except c16 at 16384³ in one job. No lower-draw win.
- **At the cap, 16384³:** 256x128 c8 goes under 1, 0.972–1.002, median 0.982 (23505901). 128x256 c8 holds in the median. It is bimodal by process: per job 6 of 8 processes read high and 2 of 8 read about 0.97; pooled 5 of 18 processes across 23505149, 23506098, 23506131, 23506132 are low.
- **Review corrections on that:** the low mode is not a cap effect. Low processes run about 90 TF/GHz at higher clock against 95–97 normally, and solo job 23506131 shows the same signature with its twin at 0% activity. It is an efficiency loss fixed per process, cause unknown [hypothesis: placement or code-object load]. "Ratio follows UMC" fails there, since low-mode processes have the lowest UMC. The solo cost of 128x256 at 16384³ (1.020 vs 1.030) is confounded by low mode; in normal mode paired 1.001, no cost. The 6144³ cost repeats (paired 0.993; 1.001 vs 1.014).
- **Limits:** all cap jobs share one node, and 23505118/119 share one node. "Solo" does not mean an idle twin; 23505118/119 metrics do not identify their card. The final bytes of `gemm_prefetch.py` never ran in a GPU job (edited after 23506192; source-equivalent by a CPU check, not re-run). `bench_gemm.py --best` still runs the older kernels.
- **Open defect, unexercised [inferred from source]:** `select_mode` accepts shapes that overflow the hand kernel's 32-bit offsets (2·max(M,N)·K ≥ 2^32 or M·N ≥ 2^31), for example 65536³, and would return wrong results silently. `hand_tile` needs a guard. Kernel code was not changed in this step.
- **Open:** six shapes at the cap (about 0.45 GPU-hours), a second node for the cap, the low-mode cause, the 6144³ efficiency gap (hb's kernel there was never logged), a TileLang pass for the schedule, clang 22, bf16, small-M.

- **Honest headline:** 0.51–0.97 against vLLM's full call and 0.56–0.97 against its stage path, on a stack vLLM cannot run on, so those ratios cross processes and compilers. Same process and runtime exists only in the image: 0.984–0.987 at T = 4096 zipf2 and still over 1.0 at T = 16 (1.08 uniform in 2 jobs, 1.09–1.11 zipf1). The 0.51 end is not a kernel result: at T = 16 zipf2 vLLM's full call (0.39–0.41 ms) is slower than its own stages (0.34 ms). Worst-pair ratio in the three jobs is 0.987, so arm order and drift do not flip a verdict; stack order was fixed in the round 1 cycles, and our T = 4096 uniform moves 5.4% between jobs.
- **Correctness [observed]:** all T, routings, stacks; ours worst 3.79e-4 against fp32, vLLM worst 5.98e-4, no non-finite values. Self-test of the landed file ran on our stack only (3.76e-4–3.79e-4).
- **What moved it [observed]:** F5 alone stays over vLLM at T = 4096: 1.035 with `torch.cat` timed (the arm that never ran before), 1.026 last row no copy, 1.022 zero row built outside the timer (23503715). The lead comes from a new down tile 64x64x128: k2 1.616 to 1.284 ms (23503738), vLLM 1.46–1.54. Gate/up 64x64x128 at 512 threads takes k1 at T = 1024 from 1.242 to 1.050 ms and loses at T = 4096. Sum at 512 threads 0.132 ms against vLLM's 0.154. Leave-one-out: swap +0.876 ms, hoist and dump +0.346, sum +0.334, v2 and no-safe +0.316, align +0.107, mask +0.022. Swap alone on base saves 1.095 ms. T = 16 weight bandwidth is 1.13x vLLM (ours over vLLM, higher is better), from 0.85x.
- **Unroll trace [observed for k1, T = 4096 only]:** the same code object behaves the same in both runtimes (23503930). The fast build issues 6-load groups; slow builds issue loads one at a time [inferred from disassembly; no counters]. Fragile: removing the mask flips it on clang 18.
- **Negative [observed]:** tiles do not carry to clang 22; the small tile ran 1.26–1.93x in the image at T = 16–256. Unroll flags do not restore k1 on clang 22 (23504481): tile A at T = 16 is 0.710 none, 0.714 threshold 20000, 0.727 threshold 0, 2.064 `-fno-unroll-loops`, against 0.369 on clang 18. bM 128 with the good down tile loses at every routing, best 1.423, 1.492, 1.549 (23504493); the shipped tile in that job reads 0.91–0.94 by cycle uniform (median 0.923), 0.958, 0.974.
- **Review's compile-only finding, untimed [observed, no GPU]:** the next lever is refuted: four bM 16 tiles with 9–10 loads are serial on clang 22, so "at most 10 loads are grouped" is not a rule. `-mllvm -misched=gcn-max-ilp` groups loads on clang 22 and clang 18 at unchanged source: tile A 20 x 1, D 18 x 1, with occupancy falling from 7 to 4 on A. `-amdgpu-sched-strategy=` aborts hipcc on clang 18, and its `gcn-` spellings are silently ignored. Forecast T = 16 uniform 0.89–0.96 and zipf1 0.90–1.007 is a hypothesis until timed. This round did not time it. Evidence `evidence/N1/skeptic_r2/`.
- **Open:** that flag; `scaling/ep_moe.py` still uses the old forms, so multi-GPU MoE has none of this; `scripts/moe_fused.py` on LUMI is the old copy; no real checkpoint or routing; no counters; self-test not run in the image.

Now a wrapper with try/finally, marker `-DGQA_FAST_N5_V2`. 23504468 `d128_4k_P32` plain arm was probably the fast kernel (max_diff exactly 0.0; 2.44e-4 after the fix in 23504747) [inferred].

Rotation is weak (sum of tag ordinals), CK always ran first in each case and was never measured late. Reciprocal on MHA P160: about +0.2%, same-process median 0.986 to 0.988; needs about +1.2% on randn and +5.5% on `big`. bN80 costs 3–6%. The claimed edge cost at bN32 is a process artefact, withdrawn.
- **Limits:** the three round 2 jobs ran back to back on one node, so use the wider bands above. MHA timed only at L = 4096. Control hash claim for assembled kernels has no saved output. Lever claim "every number-backed lever is spent" is too strong: MHA idle factor 1.063 at P160 and 1.228 at P192 is not attacked.
- **Open:** MHA grid fill by L, d128 4k–16k, real inputs, batch timing, CK's clock, 1k and clang 18 timing with the V2 module, six-input repeat.

### 2026-11-06 · N3: decode against vLLM (`evidence/N3/`; no RESULTS.md)
Ratios: vLLM ms over our ms, higher is better. % is share of nominal 1.6 TB/s. Random data, not a real KV cache. Review corrections applied; they change the frame.
- **Jobs:** round 1: 23503824, 23503858, 23503876, 23503950, 23503994, 23503998, 23504006, 23504036, 23504061, 23504075, 23504110, 23504120, 23504123, 23504152, 23504164, 23504182, 23504284, 23504328, 23504371, 23504372, 23504388, 23504391 (1473 s = 0.41 GPU-hours); review 23504466 (0.006). Round 2: 23504499, 23504555, 23504570, 23504571, 23504605, 23504656, 23504672 (1409 s = 0.39, budget about 0.1); review 23504738, 23504743 (0.033). Total 3022 s = 0.84 (sacct). 23503994, 23503998 failed at load (bad build); 23504371 failed on an `IntImm` bug, fixed; 23504555 crashed in reload code.
- **The right baseline [review; source plus direct calls, no live server inspected]:** vLLM's ROCm default is ROCM_ATTN, layout LHBNC (K x-packed, V D-major). At head size 256 `use_custom` is False, so decode runs `kernel_paged_attention_2d` through `chunked_prefill_paged_decode` ("roc"). TRITON_ATTN defaults to LBNHC ("tri"). The [blocks, Hkv, page, 2D] layout used by the landed kernel and the "uni" arm is LBHNC, reached only with VLLM_KV_CACHE_LAYOUT=HND. So round 2's "real layout" label is wrong as a statement about the default, and using the landed kernel requires a layout choice. Landed has not run on LBNHC or LHBNC, and the cache write path was not timed.
- **Measured, ms, jobs 23504738, 23504743, image stack, base lengths, all correct to at most 2.93e-4:**

  | Shape | Landed, LBHNC | uni, LBHNC | tri, LBNHC | roc, LHBNC |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 8 x 32k | 0.84 | 2.78 | 2.79 | 5.38–5.46 |
  | 32 x 8k | 0.85 | 0.98 | 0.99 | 1.50–1.51 |
  | 64 x 4k | 0.84 | 1.01 | 0.97–0.98 | 1.25–1.27 |

  Over the 24 cells of the six shapes and four length patterns: against roc 1.35–9.91x, against tri 0.94–4.95x.
- **Against uni on the same tensor (23504656 / 23504672, median of 3 cycles, ratio; landed %):** 8 x 32k 3.32/3.33 base, 2.79/2.81 full, 3.76/3.78 partial, 4.94/4.87 skew (65–70%). 16 x 16k 1.82, 1.58–1.59, 1.85, 2.74–2.75 (64–75%). 32 x 8k 1.16/1.21, 1.09/1.15, 1.34/1.37, 1.71/1.72 (62–73%). 64 x 4k 1.22, 1.20–1.21, 1.19–1.20, 1.14–1.16 (63–71%). 32 x 1k 0.99–1.00, 1.03–1.04, 1.04–1.05, 1.40–1.41 (49–61%). 64 x 1k 1.22–1.24, 1.25, 1.21–1.23, 0.98–1.00 (48–69%). Same landed form at 32 x 8k full spans 1.08–1.27 across jobs and seeds, so these are draws, not bounds.
- **Standing:** lead against the out-of-box default by at least 1.35x, conditional on the layout change [inferred]. Against TRITON_ATTN not everywhere: 64 x 1k skew is a small standing loss, median 0.93 over 8 length draws (range 0.87–1.05) against tri; 64 x 2k skew against uni median 0.99. 32 x 1k base is a tie against uni. Plan criterion of 50% of HBM at batch ≥ 8: held on the 1 GB shapes (62–75%), not on short skew (48–49%).
- **What landed [observed]:** `gqa_decode.py` `decode_partial_paged_v2` (all 8 `dwordx4` K+V loads in one round; `rc` one division per row; taper 44 then 12 pages), `decode_combine_v2`, `decode_partial_paged_v3` (length-aware splits, for 256 pages or more), `paged_kernels` (per-shape choice, uniform S=27 at batch 16, uniform splits under 700 blocks), `tail="kvg"` NaN-tail fix. Load rounds: k1 1.0043 to 0.8033 ms at batch 32, checksums identical. BT must hold valid page ids, since bounds checks are off.
- **Refuted [observed]:** the 1.73x forecast (measured gain 1.12–1.32x by shape and stack). Random pages as the limit (1209 vs 1173 GB/s). 256 threads one round (864 GB/s), TileLang 64-thread form (555 GB/s; hand form 1.1–1.4% above landed, not landed). Best load-only is 1287–1309 GB/s at S ≥ 26, single jobs; do not call it a cap, since at S = 13 a math kernel (k1 0.7866) beat load-only ld3 (0.8025). Removing further barriers adds almost nothing. k2 is 1.2% of the step at S = 13. Correction to 5.8 [inferred]: 128 threads is 3 blocks/CU, not 4, since TileLang asks 17408 B LDS (peak blocks 5.89 vs 7.86 per bin, ratio only).
- **Modes [observed]:** K/V placement flips separate-tensor v2f between about 0.797 and 0.825 ms, by gap between K and V (cause unknown, channel conflict hypothesis). On one kept allocation a further 9–10% flip exists on full lengths (23504571), cause unknown. So 1.25x at batch 32 was the fast mode; on layout LBHNC batch 32 base is 1.16–1.21x. First bench after a fresh allocation runs 3–9% slow.
- **Correctness:** landed 2.7e-4–2.8e-4 vs fp64, vLLM 2.9e-4; partial last pages run and pass (23504466); stale NaN tail now 2.75e-4, was all NaN. The old baseline kernel keeps the defect.
- **Open:** landed on LBNHC and LHBNC; K-then-V staging to recover the 0 to 13% (time) layout cost; flip diagnostic; short skew at batch 64; live cache check; LUMI-stack 128 x 2k stuck at 1.03 ms and 1 x 4k at a 0.067 ms floor, unexplained. `gqa_decode.py` and `vllm_paged_baseline.py:81` still carry the wrong "real layout" and "ROCm default backend" labels.

### 2026-11-06 · C1, C9, C7: TileLang compiler fixes (`evidence/C1/RESULTS.md`, `tilelang-patches/`)
Ratios: ours over hb, throughput, higher is better. Clang 18 unless stated. C1's direct harness uses fixed-exponent data [inferred from `hbm3.cpp`, as E1], so these understate randn ratios per N4.
- **Jobs:** builds 23503722, 23503813 (CPU; the 453 s build billed 64 CPUs, 8.05 CPU-hours). GPU round 1: 23503968, 23503970, 23503992, 23504030, 23504096, 23504099, 23504117, 23504161, 23504162, 23504347, 23504348 (3057 s = 0.849 GPU-hours). Round 2: 23504514, 23504515, 23504573, 23504574, 23504576, 23504580, 23504611, 23504612 (1226 s = 0.34). Total 4283 s = 1.19 (sacct). The final `libtilelang.so` has no job or log (presumably built outside Slurm; `.ninja_log` kept). 23504574 reports TIMEOUT after all 8 DONE lines.
- **Patches:** 0001 occupancy from `T.annotate_min_blocks_per_sm`, `waves_per_eu = ceil(blocks * ceil(threads/wave_size) / 4)`; 0002 uint8 Broadcast at 4, 8, 16 lanes; 0003 clear messages on small tiles (the 16-row rejection is correct, not a bug); 0004 `occupancy_report()`. 24 new tests pass on GPU on the final tree, 97 existing in 4 AMD test files pass; full suite not run. Series applies in order on upstream main 827a7799, not built there; CUDA build of 0001 got a syntax check only.
- **C1 without a fork [observed]:** `tilelang_callback_hip_postproc` gives byte-identical source on the stock wheel and the same speed. The kernel cache cannot see the hook or `HIPCC_COMPILE_FLAGS_APPEND`, so arms need their own cache or a `compile_flags` entry. An annotation under a python `if` is silently dropped.
- **JIT numbers [observed]:** b25632 annotated 8192³ 100.98, 103.33 TF, was 53; 16384³ 90.51, 95.01, was 41. Ratio 0.89–0.91 and 0.77–0.82 across 5 jobs. Same kernel goes from 143 VGPR + 128 AGPR, Occupancy 1, to 166 + 0, Occupancy 3.
- **Landed through JIT, no fork:** `bench_gemm.py --best` with E1's staged bodies (job 23504612 plus the two s13 jobs). Direct, jobs without a twin: 4096³ rr25632w2 0.968–0.978; 8192³ about 0.90–0.91 typically, 0.94–0.947 in 2 of about 10 processes; 17408 0.936–0.939; 32768 rr12864w3 1.059–1.063; 16384³ b25632a2 0.72–0.80, typically 0.79. N4's hand kernel now beats all of these at those shapes. `--best` on the merged local `bench_gemm.py` and `gemm_prefetch.py` was never run.
- **Load gaps, new finding [observed]:** `s_nop` between consecutive global loads speeds b12864: +12.9% at 8192³, 83 to 92.9 TF through the flag `-mllvm -amdgpu-max-memory-clause=1`. Alignment control 0.999, so the gap itself matters. It explains `w3`'s gain on that tile. Still loses to staged kernels. Dose lever closed by the stop rule: rr25632w2 at 4096³ −0.3 to −1.3%, 17408 0.0 to +1.0%. rr12864w3 `s_nop 7` at 32768 +0.4 to +1.7%, needs asm post-processing, not landed. `gfx90a:xnack-` removes all gaps (compile only). Clang 22: the flag's gain does not carry (+0.8, +1.3, +0.3, −0.5%, one job).
- **Two-state behaviour, unexplained [observed]:** our kernels swing 4–5% at 8192³ and 17408 and 9% at 16384³ between processes and within one, while hb holds. Experiment D and neighbour explanations are open; `hipDeviceMallocContiguous` was never tried. Allocating and freeing other buffers first does nothing (23504576). "All no-twin jobs clean" is false: they overlapped other jobs on the node.
- **Open:** contiguous-allocation test, CUDA build, `hip.kernels_output_dir`.

### 2026-11-06 · N4 and C1 s19: GEMM placement (`evidence/N4/RESULTS.md`, `evidence/C1/RESULTS.md`)
Ours over hipBLASLt (hb), throughput, higher is better, same process. Hand kernels are hand-scheduled inline asm from `hand_source`, **not compiler output**. Review corrections applied.
- **Jobs:** round 1 N4 23508438, 23508521, 23508569, 23508611, 23508622, 23508639, 23508697, 23508774, 23508775, 23508861, 23508942, 23508994, 23509045, 23509056 (3.768 GPU-hours); C1 s19 23508522, 23508523 (0.400). Round 2 N4 23509140, 23509177, 23509178, 23509214, 23509215, 23509216, 23509217, 23509218, 23509312, 23509325 (1.156). 23509219 cancelled before start. 23508774's `arms_*` and `comp_*` files read offset 0 only through a shift-list error; 23508861 replaces them. Arm order is in `evidence/N4/raw/`.
- **Low mode [observed]:** flips within a process, 16 of 48 reads low after reallocation (23508521). Reallocating A flips it in 10 of 24 steps, B in 10 of 24, C in 0 of 24, nothing in 3 of 96 (23508611). Shifting both A and B by the same amount leaves the state in 29 of 32 reads (23508639, without the 4 no-shift controls). hb stays flat.
- **Cause, as corrected:** the low mode depends on the A-B address relationship, including higher bits. A − B = **exactly 128 mod 256** lifts it at every read so far; it is an escape, not the cause. All 816 unshifted reads have A and B 2 MiB aligned, so the flips come from higher bits [inferred; physical addresses not read]. Offsets 144, 160, 192, 224, 240 do not give the gain (23508774). At 16384³ other shifts also help: 8192–24576 read 1.049, 256 reads 1.039, against 1.058 at 128 (23508697). Hardware reason open; interleave is a hypothesis.
- **At the cap, 128x256, B at offset 128, 8 GCDs, 2 nodes (23508775, 23508942), medians, 16 reads each, 0 of 256 reads below 1 [computed]:** 16384³ 1.047, 1.045; 6144³ 1.016, 1.023; 8192³ 1.070, 1.068; 12288³ 1.057, 1.057; 4096³ 1.089, 1.078; 4096x17408x5120 1.057, 1.057; 32768x1024x2048 1.089, 1.093; 16384x4096x4096 1.056, 1.060. Range 1.008–1.104. Offset 0 in the same jobs: 1.016, 0.998–1.005, 1.030–1.036, 1.042–1.044, 1.055, 1.045–1.049, 1.057–1.059, 1.035–1.038. These are **8 tested shapes**, not every shape `hand_tile` accepts. 5632x3200x4096 has no 128x256 form; 256x128 c8 reads 1.035 unplaced at the cap, one job (23508622). Arm-order rotation is incomplete but position effects are at most 0.5% and hb reads the same at positions 0 and 2 [computed]. Package power 481–499 W, throttle flags 0.
- **Through the real script, placed, at the cap (final bytes a0c1c3a8, 23509325; 23508942 on b587c02e):** 16384³ 1.043 [1.031–1.067], 1.044 [1.042–1.056]; 6144³ 1.024 [1.012–1.028], 1.022 [1.003–1.047], on 2 different nodes. 8192³ 1.072 [1.066–1.080] and 12288³ 1.051 [1.041–1.070]: 1 job each, below the repeat rule. rel_err equals hb's.
- **Script, placed, solo, 9 shapes:** 1.026–1.089 (23509312, no other project job on its node), 1.004–1.100 (23509218, shared with other N4 GCDs). **Unplaced:** 16384³ 1.015 at the cap [0.978–1.022], 6144³ 1.003 [0.996–1.008] (23508622); script solo on `38d37465` 0.999–1.000 and 0.988–0.990 (23509056). So plain tensors do not reliably beat hb at those two shapes without `place_b`.
- **`place_b` cost:** one copy of B is 0.92 ms at 16384³, about 1.3% of a GEMM there, and the fraction scales as 1/M. Per-call copying at the cap nets about 1.033 at 16384³, a gain; 1.040 at 8192³, a tie; 1.029 at 4096³ against 1.055 unplaced, a loss; 0.986 at 6144³, below hb [computed, 6144³ and 4096³ copy costs extrapolated]. Only a long-lived B, or B allocated placed, keeps the table's numbers.
- **Rule costs:** `HAND_128X256` trades about +2% at the cap for 2–3% solo (12288³ 1.055–1.059 to 1.031–1.034; 4096x17408x5120 1.057–1.060 to 1.040–1.045, across jobs). At low K with `placed=True`, 128x256 reads below unplaced 256x128: 4096x4096x192 1.186 against 1.246; x512 1.044 against 1.054–1.057; x1024 1.032–1.043 against 1.047–1.049; 2560x2560x256 1.228–1.239 against 1.244–1.249 (one job and arm order per shape). 256x128 on a placed B not read.
