**No kernel-against-vendor ratio changes here.** Review corrections applied, over the first report's text. All OFI numbers are a **hand-written adapter** (`scaling/ofi_v6_adapter.c`) over LUMI's aws-ofi-rccl 1.4.0 through a `libamdhip64` shim on ROCm 7.2.3: not a compiler or vendor result.
- **Jobs and GCD-hours [sacct]:** MoE 23515890, 23516010, 23516011, 23516410, 23516411, 23516705, 23516765 = 21.28 against a plan of 1.41, almost all hangs holding 32 GCDs; the clean ladder alone is 2.63. vLLM 23515891, 23516861, 23518326 = 7.49. Comm and repeats 23525495, 23525496, 23525497 = 0.78. OFI serving 23525737, 23525738, 23525969, 23526306 = 6.38.
- **MoE ladder, landed md5 9ad0c345, clang 18, synthetic routing, 1.3x padding added by `ep_moe.py`, weak scaling with 256 experts in total [computed from raw].** T = 2048, 8 GCDs: efficiency 76.4% in both jobs with their own W = 1 (23516765, 23516411), 76.4 to 76.7% over 3 jobs against a pooled W = 1 (review's 76.3 and 39.4 to 39.6 carried a 0.1-point slip). Old kernels 99.0 to 99.2%. Throughput rises anyway: 3.22 M against 2.71 M tok/s. 16 GCDs 39.4 to 39.7%; 32 GCDs 25.8 to 26.6%. 8 to 32 GCDs gives 4.345 / 3.219 = 1.35x throughput for 4x the GCDs.
- **Reading [inferred].** "99% inside a node" does not hold for landed kernels. The fall comes from a faster baseline, not worse communication: with W = 1 compute held at W = 8, efficiency reads about 74% old and 64% land, so the old 99% was compute savings cancelling 2.2 ms of added dispatch and combine. Compute is under 12% of the 32-GCD step. Keep expert parallelism inside a node. `land` is landed kernel code in a non-landed call (one row per pair, no landed `topk_sum`, and at T = 512 the large tile), per `ep_moe.py`.
- **vLLM TP=4, two nodes, sockets (the image's fallback) [computed, cross-job, single-run figures, not headlines]:** two over one: batch 1 0.56 to 0.65; batch 8 0.57; batch 32 0.36 to 0.40; prefill 0.20 to 0.23. All-to-all 256 MB 7.0 to 7.8 GB/s against 190 GB/s in a node. Same job 23525969 round 0, socket n = 1: 0.70, 0.37, 0.19 to 0.20.
- **OFI serving [computed].** OFI over sockets on prefill: 2.9 to 4.0x across jobs, with one same-job pair at 4.3 to 4.6x whose top end rests on a socket prefill (702 tok/s) 28 to 31% below other socket runs, so **"about 3 to 4x across jobs" is the robust statement**. Batch 1 1.20 to 1.37x, batch 8 1.34 to 1.48x, batch 32 2.24x (one pair; 1.70 to 2.18x across jobs), long prompt 2.73 to 3.97x in time, comm all-to-all 256 MB 8.3 to 16.6x (61.8 to 124.4 GB/s). OFI over one node: prefill 0.86 to 0.87 same job against a cold-compile arm with a different hash, 0.65 to 0.81 across jobs against 23516861; batch 1 0.75 to 0.83; batch 32 0.67 to 0.83. **Unstated confound:** every OFI row reaching prefill or batch 32 ran at NCCL INFO; the across-jobs columns compare it with WARN arms, and OFI prefill at WARN was never measured.
- **Stability [observed].** 12 two-node attempts: sockets 8 (2 finished, 5 hung, 1 died at startup), OFI 4 (3 finished, 1 hung). One-node arms finished 2 of 2. sacct says COMPLETED on jobs with hung arms; read `## <arm> client rc=`. Per burst of 32: socket 5 of 12 stalled, OFI 1 of 10, one node 0 of 6, Fisher p about 0.16, so the two transports **cannot be told apart** [computed]. The stall is in the first forward pass of the burst: no prompt tokens of the 32 requests were counted in all 5 hangs of the last round. Last engine snapshot differs (Running 1 in 3, Running 32 in 2), so "same signature" overstates it [inferred]. Mixed compile state, sockets, INFO logging and 4 GCDs per node are not necessary conditions; `THINK_ON` 36 is not a predictor. `shm_broadcast` messages are not hang-specific (3 during a healthy cold start). Cause open.
- **Output [observed].** Correctness is established at batch 1 (hash `a12e8aec31e5` in every arm but one cold one-node arm, `59c9136d10b4`), needle, tool and think checks, and comm `a2a_bad` and `reduce_bad` 0. Batch 8 and 32 hashes move round to round in every arm and prefill rows carry none, so "output right" is that narrow.
- **Trap [observed; keying inferred].** vLLM's compile cache keys on rank but stores device ordinals, so a TP=4 cache from one layout breaks on another (`invalid device ordinal`).
- **Other [computed].** Paper's 87% against 83.9% at 32 GCDs: 14.017 / 16.07 = 87.2% on the step, 84.6% on the phase sum, so 2.6 of 3.3 points are the formula; job 23499049's log is on LUMI only. MoE and vLLM cross-node results are on different transports and images and do not compare. py-spy, gdb and pystack are absent from the image; Python's faulthandler imports.
- **Open:** the hang (stack dump via faulthandler and `kill -ABRT` on client rc 124, about 0.54 GCD-hours per try); a warm same-job baseline at WARN, about 1.7; FI_CXI tuning or a plugin built for ROCm 7.2.3. Replicas remain the likely route for serving [hypothesis].

Eager, other seeds: Hkv 2 32x8k skew seed 989 0.965-0.967. Counts "2 of 24" and "10 of 36" below 1 are seed 988 only.
- **`pickg` is not deployable [read from source, computed].** It is a kernel fitted to one exact length set, then replayed. FULL-graph serving captures once, and the plugin does not use the picker. So graph ratios compare kernels and probably flatter ours.
- **3D against 2D on replay [computed].** 3D is up to 9.4x faster at 1x8k. 3D is slower at 32x1k Hkv 4 base 1.25, short x32 1.58-1.59, short x8 1.21-1.22, short x1 1.07-1.11, Hkv 4 32x2k base 1.08, 32x1k mid 1.02.
- **Does it explain V1? [computed; 3D in serving inferred, strongly, no server instrumented].** Per-rank model 16 layers x kernel ms against V1's step:

The 3D model lands within 0.8-2.2 ms of observed; the 2D model misses 8k by 9.2-10.7 ms; at 2k x32 they barely differ, so only 8k discriminates. Launch cost `16(pick - pickg)` is +0.20 to +0.30 on short cells and 2k x1 but -0.07 to -0.08 at 2k x8, 2k x32, 8k x1, 8k x8, so the first report's "0.23 to 0.3" does not generalise.
- **Conclusion.** Headroom ours over 3D is -0.31 to +0.26 ms per step, at most 0.8% of a step, within V1's noise; Triton 3D attention is at most 1.84-1.98 ms of 46-50 ms, so a zero-time kernel gives at most about 4%, **in V1's eight cells only**. The kernel gap explains the lack of gain there. **It does not explain the plugin's measured loss: residual -0.04 to +1.25 ms; at 8k x1 the model predicts a small gain, so all of the observed loss is unexplained.** `igs128_tri`, with no eager launch and no PIECEWISE, still reads +0.18 to +1.06 ms over `s_tri` outside short x1 (short x1 -0.27..+0.28), largest at 2k x1; the S = 128 form may be why [hypothesis], not raced. The 0.8-2.2 ms gap on `rocm - tri` is unexplained too. At 8x32k and 32x8k, `16 x tri3g` is 3.95-6.96 ms, no step was measured, ours over Triton reaches about 0.6 ms in base and mid draws at those shapes, so the serving conclusion is not established there.
- **State.** "Kernel at least 1 against Triton" is not met at these cells. V1 stands with a kernel-level reason. Cache copy on LUMI removed; `exp/TRI3D` keeps scripts and raw. No landed kernel changed.

### 2026-11-09 · S5 two-node hang: all-rank stack and RCCL proxy dumps (`evidence/S5/raw/hangdump-20261109/`, `scaling/vllm_mn_dump.sbatch`, `scaling/hangdump/`)
**No kernel-against-vendor ratio changes. Sockets only (the image's fallback); OFI was not rerun.**
- **Setup.** `vllm_mn_dump.sbatch` is `vllm_mn.sbatch` (md5 a2dbef12) plus: client in the background; a watchdog that fires when vLLM's ring-buffer wait message appears after the client starts (`VLLM_RINGBUFFER_WARNING_INTERVAL=30`) or no client row arrives for 150 s; then `hangdump/dump_node.sh` on both nodes at once: ps/top/rocm-smi, py-spy 0.4.3 `--native` (static wheel, run from the host against the image's processes), RCCL `NCCL_PROXY_DUMP_SIGNAL=12` (SIGUSR2), faulthandler via `hangdump/sitecustomize.py` (SIGUSR1), py-spy again 10 s later, then gdb. NCCL INFO with `INIT,NET,PROXY,COLL` into per-process files; only the last 400 lines per rank of the two hung attempts are kept. Same model, flags, `mn-tp4` AOT cache, 2 GCDs per node, 120 GB per node as S5. CPU preflight 23561720 checked py-spy, gdb and faulthandler against an image process. **py-spy works from the host; librccl is stripped, so gdb and py-spy native frames inside RCCL carry no names.** Job 23562267's first attempt used dump order v1 (gdb with full symbols before the signals, no RSS sampler); the later jobs used the final scripts.
- **Jobs [sacct].** 23562267 (3 attempts, 24:32), 23562914 (2 attempts, 17:14), 23564023 (1 attempt with `ROUNDS=12`, 17:22), all dev-g. **4 GCDs x 3,548 s = 3.94 GCD-hours** [computed]. dev-g counted 56 CPUs per node for `--cpus-per-task=28`, so sacct `billing` is 112 against 56 for the same request on small-g (23526306); billed by that, about 7.9 GCD-hour-equivalents, still inside the 12. Preflight: CPU, 60 s.
- **Counts [observed].** 6 two-node socket attempts: **2 hung, 4 finished.** Batch-32 bursts: 22 started, 20 completed, 2 stalled [computed] (S5 had 5 of 12). Hang 1: attempt 1, at the first batch-32 burst. Hang 2: the 12-round attempt, the 9th burst (round 8). Batch-1 hash `a12e8aec31e5` in every attempt. Both stalls fired the watchdog 30 s into the stall; dumps took 25 to 80 s; py-spy pass 1 and pass 2 were identical apart from one output-copy thread.
- **Where it blocks: the TP collective path inside a 32-request prefill step, not the Python scheduler [observed].** In both hangs the step is an eager (beyond graph capture) batch of 581 tokens: every rank's COLL log ends with 129 AllReduces of 2,974,720 bf16 (581 x 5120; 1 + 2 per layer) on vLLM's pynccl communicator, then the logits AllGather of 32 rows on torch's ProcessGroupNCCL communicator. EngineCore waits in `collective_rpc ... dequeue` for worker output; the API server is idle. vLLM's message queue delivered the step to all 4 workers in both hangs.
  - **Hang 1 (23562267): missing rank 2.** Rank 2 (node 1, local rank 0) logged 17,415 AllReduces and 388 AllGathers; ranks 0, 1 and 3 logged 17,416 and 389. py-spy: rank 2's main thread is *inside* `NCCLLibrary.ncclAllReduce` (pynccl, `GroupCoordinator._all_reduce_out_place`, piecewise compiled graph, `Qwen3NextModel.forward`), after RCCL's tuning lines for its 128th AllReduce of the step. Ranks 0, 1 and 3 are idle in `dequeue`; rank 0's output-copy thread waits in `Event.synchronize`. RCCL proxy dump, AllGather communicator (op 774 = AllGather 388, the one torch's watchdog later named: "work 388, last enqueued 389, last completed 387"): rank 0 receives from rank 3 on all 4 channels, posted 8, received 0, 21.7 M retries; rank 1 sends to rank 2, posted 8, transmitted 0, 68 M retries. GPUs at 100% on both nodes. **Rank 2's RSS was 76 GB** (top at the stall; rank 3 4.3 GB; healthy runs hold rank 2 flat at 9.3 GB in the RSS sampler). The v1 gdb pass then pushed node 1 over 120 GB and the step was OOM-killed, so rank 2's own proxy dump was lost.
  - **Hang 2 (23564023): no missing host call.** All 4 ranks logged identical counts (44,248 AllReduces, 3,566 AllGathers) and all 4 main threads are idle in `dequeue`; rank 0's output-copy thread waits in `Event.synchronize`. Proxy dumps from both nodes, AllGather communicator, op 7128 (AllGather 3,565 of 3,566; 7130 queued): rank 0 receives from rank 3, rank 1 sends to rank 2, rank 2 receives from rank 1, rank 3 sends to rank 0, **every one at step 0** (posted 8, transmitted or received 0). GPUs at 100%. RSS normal (local rank 0 10.7 GB, local rank 1 5.4 GB).
- **Reading [inferred].** In both hangs the logits AllGather never moved a byte: the senders' GPU kernels never produced data, while every GPU is busy, so the AllGather kernels are not running. They are most likely queued behind AllReduce kernels of the same step that cannot finish. Hang 1 explains why: rank 2 never launched its last AllReduces. Hang 2 has all launches issued, so its stall is on the GPU or in the AllReduce communicator's proxy. **The AllReduce communicator's proxy state was not captured:** RCCL's dump signal prints only the last-created communicator. Socket transport is not implicated beyond being the transport in use; S5's OFI hang (1 of 4) had the same engine signature. The 76 GB host growth on the blocked rank is one observation and is not a necessary condition.
- **Mitigation: none tested** (the 6-attempt cap was reached when the second hang was captured). Cheap candidates in order [hypothesis]: (1) `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` raised (e.g. 8 or 16), since HIP maps streams onto 4 hardware queues by default and two communicators on different streams (compute stream AllReduce, ProcessGroupNCCL stream AllGather) can serialize in different orders on different GPUs; (2) pipeline parallel across nodes (`--pipeline-parallel-size 2 --tensor-parallel-size 2`), which removes cross-node TP collectives; (3) keep prefill steps inside captured graph sizes (`--max-num-batched-tokens` at or below the largest capture size), since both hangs were in eager 581-token steps. Each needs about 12 bursts or more per arm to read: at 2 of 22 here, a clean arm of 12 bursts has about a 1 in 3 chance by luck alone [computed]. Replicas remain the low-risk route [hypothesis].
- **Next if continued.** A second, separately created dump of the AllReduce communicator (create pynccl last, or set `NCCL_PROXY_DUMP_SIGNAL` per communicator via a patched RCCL), `RCCL_KERNEL_COLL_TRACE_ENABLE=1` for the failing step only (the string is in the image's librccl), then the `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` arm.
- **Files.** `evidence/S5/raw/hangdump-20261109/`: three job `.out` files, and per hang the client rows, py-spy pass 1 for all processes, ps/top/rocm-smi, RSS, faulthandler files, proxy dumps, NCCL tails and gzipped serve logs. `make_release.sh` does not list the new scripts.

### 2026-11-09 · PREFILL-MAX round 1: transposed-V LDS and a register prefetch land in `gqa_fa` for D 128 (form JTP) and D 256 (`vt`) (`evidence/PREFILL-MAX/`, `raw/summary.txt`)
**Landed: `tilelang/gqa_fa.py` and LUMI `scripts/gqa_fa.py` are e3caf113 (was ffc58f6a, copy kept in `exp/PFMAX/src/old_ffc58f6a_gqa_fa.py`).** Ours over same-process vendor, throughput, higher is better; vendor read = mean of pre and post. Wheel 0.1.15 on the vLLM image (clang 22), CK = flash-attn 2.8.3. B 1, G 6, Hkv 4, causal. Clock = mean `current_gfxclk` sampled by amdsmi every ~2 ms during each timed loop, on the GCD matched by PCI bus [observed]. **Corrected 9 Nov (round 3): `current_gfxclk` reports the 1650 MHz target, not a measured clock (1812 of 2550 readings in this round's raw files are exactly 1650.0; DECODE-MAX's clock64 probe read ~935 MHz, round 2's GRBM estimate ~1.1 GHz). Every "% at clock" figure below is unreliable and is not a hardware standing; only % of nominal (TF / 191.5) stands.** % of peak = TF / 191.5 (1700 MHz) and TF / (191.5 x MHz / 1700) [computed].
- **Lever [observed, compiled].** The PV GEMM read V from LDS two bytes at a time: form J had 128 `ds_read_u16` (+72 `v_perm`) per K step for 64 MFMAs, the d256 fast kernel the same 128. V now goes to LDS transposed, [d][key pair] dwords with an XOR swizzle (d128: d x 32 + (c ^ 2 (d & 15)); bN 32: d x 16 + (c ^ 2 ((d >> 1) & 7))), so each MFMA's A operand is one 8-byte read (compiler: 16 `ds_read2st64_b64`). V's global-load thread map changes so every LDS write instruction spreads over the banks; an empty asm keeps address math in the loop (without it JT took 150 VGPRs and occupancy 3). Form JTP adds a register prefetch of the next K/V step before the PV GEMM: 126 VGPRs (J 119), occupancy 4 kept, 1094 instructions (J 1156). d256 `vt`: 151 VGPRs (158), 1070 instructions (1186). Code: `_jtp_template()`/`_JTP_SRC` (text edits of `_J_SRC`), `_vt_edit` (after `_fast_edit`, checks the generated V index numerically against the assumed map), `d256_tile`, `gqa_flash_fast(..., vt=)`, `gqa_flash_j(L, form)`. CLI `gqa_fa.py L auto 256` added.
- **Research races [computed; 2 jobs each, opposite order, 3 inputs randn0, big, ar; 0 errors, repeats 20/20, 0 non-finite].** d128 (23566624, 23566625, 23566958, 23566959), over the landed dispatch ffc58f6a, 13 lengths 1024-16384: J 0.987-1.074, JT 1.025-1.072 (4096-16384), **JTP 1.011-1.126, every cell**. (Corrected 9 Nov: JTP combines two levers, transposed V and the register prefetch; JT, transposed V alone, was raced only at 4096-16384, so at 1024-3584 the two levers were never separated.) d256 P32 bN32 768 threads (23567063, 23567064): **`vt` over the same tile 1.016-1.058, every cell**, L 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384.
- **Landing races through the candidate module [computed; corrected 9 Nov, round 3].** d128 six inputs (23567144 fwd, 23567145 rev; module 7f13cc5e, whose d128 code equals e3caf113). **No length has an opposite-order pair in this race:** 23567144 has readings only at 1024-8192 (10240-16384 failed, 48 failed tl records), 23567145 only at 14336 and 16384 (the other 15 lengths failed, 180 failed tl records). Cause: the module was replaced the module file on disk while the jobs ran and every later compile raised (TileLang re-reads the source). The earlier text ("1.012-1.140 ... every cell of both jobs", "only 10240 and 12288 missing") was wrong; the 1.140 upper bound comes from the final-module jobs 23567223/25. As landed, 4608, 7168 and 14336 had one job each and 10240 none; round 3 (below) races them and the other untimed lengths in both orders. Final module e3caf113 (23567223, 23567225): d256 new over old 1.014-1.057 at 1024, 2048, 3072, 4096, 6144, 8192, 12288, 16384 (randn0, self, big, ar); d128 1.021-1.140 at 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384 (randn0, big). Module code objects have the same instruction streams as the raced research bytes (JTP and `vt` at 8192, `icmp.py`).
- **Standing against CK [computed from the raw jsonl; table corrected 9 Nov, round 3].** Per-cell ranges (min-max over inputs and jobs), not means. % nominal = TF / 191.5. The "% at clock" column was dropped: the amdsmi value is the 1650 MHz target, not a measured clock (see the header). - **Negatives [computed, same jobs].** JTPX (softmax scale folded into a base-2 exponent, FMA + `v_exp_f32`): a tie at 1024 (JTPX/JTP 0.995-1.009, corrected 9 Nov) and below JTP from 1536 up (0.961-0.996 at 1536-6144 and 12288-16384; 8192 0.979-1.000), closed. JTPS (`s_setprio` 1 around both MFMA blocks), 23567286, 23567287: 0.995-1.013 of JTP, a tie, closed.
- **Speed win with an accuracy cost, not landed [computed].** JTPR (P cast by `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32`, round toward zero): 1.010-1.017 of JTP at 4096, 8192, 16384, every cell of both jobs; reaches 1.008 of CK at 8192 in one cell. `rel_vs_fp64` rises from 2.57e-4 to 4.66e-4 (CK 4.67e-4; limit 2e-3). Real inputs already reached 4.06e-3 with the old kernel (N5 rounds 3-4), so this needs a decision on the accuracy limit first [decision open].
- **Counters, d128 8192, JTP vs CK, rocprofv3 (23567230), diagnostic [observed; one process, median per dispatch, profiled runs serialize].** `SQ_VALU_MFMA_BUSY_CYCLES` 1.62e9 vs 1.64e9 and `GRBM_GUI_ACTIVE` 4.39e6 vs 4.36e6: matrix-core busy time is equal. Ours issues 2.0x the MFMA instructions (5.07e7 vs 2.56e7; same flops, so CK uses 32x32x8 [inferred]), 2.3x VALU (2.49e8 vs 1.09e8), 2.1x LDS instructions (3.49e7 vs 1.64e7), 14x `SQ_WAIT_INST_LDS` (8.5e7 vs 6.0e6), 2x waves; `SQ_LDS_BANK_CONFLICT` 0 for ours vs 5.1e7 for CK. The remaining gap is issue overhead and LDS wait, not bank conflicts or MFMA time [inferred].
- **Correctness [computed].** `rel_vs_fp64` at most 2.74e-4 (new) and 2.72e-4 (old) up to 8192, on 384 sampled rows above 8192 (exact fp64 for those rows) at most 2.59e-4; CK at most 4.72e-4. Repeats 20/20 everywhere, 0 non-finite.
- **Dispatch identity [observed, `raw/chk.txt`].** `d128_tile` old against new over L 1-20000, B 1-2, clang 18 and 22, and `mha_tile` over Hkv 4 and 8: 79,759 of 80,000 equal; the 241 changes are clang 22, B 1, L 1024-16384 with L % 64 == 0, all now form JTP. The landed `_JTP_SRC` equals the raced template byte for byte. `d256_tile` is new (no D 256 rule existed); `gqa_flash_fast(..., vt=False)` keeps every old compile. Landed CLI (23567339, single reads, path check only): L 1000 rows 128 bN32 (old path), 2048-16384 form JTP, d256 `vt` at 2048, 8192, 16384, MHA unchanged; errors at most 2.6e-4.
- **Not tested.** Clang 18 (JTP and `vt` are clang 22 only), B > 1, L not a multiple of 64 (d128) or 32 (d256), L outside 1024-16384, real captured inputs, end to end.
- **Lesson.** Never replace a module file while a job that compiles from it runs: TileLang re-reads the source at compile time.
- **GPU use [observed, sacct ElapsedRaw x GCDs].** 14 jobs, 1645 s = **0.46 GCD-hours**. Compiles ran on the login node.
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) A 32x32x8 form of J (32 query rows per wave, S^T in 32x32 tiles, P stays in registers as the B operand, V^T as the A operand) to halve MFMA and LDS instruction counts, as the counters point to; about 190 VGPRs, so occupancy 2 with 4-wave blocks. (2) pkrtz after a decision on accuracy. (3) The same `vt` edit for MHA tiles and rows 128 bN32 (d128 below 1024, L % 64 != 0).

### 2026-11-09 · GEMM-MAX round 1: full-fill tiles flip the three losing shapes; range-checked small-M landed; 32x32x8 negative (`evidence/GEMM-MAX/RESULTS.md`)
Ours over hipBLASLt (hb) through torch, throughput, higher is better, same process, rotated; true 2MNK. **Hand asm launched through TileLang, not a compiler result.** Error against fp64 equals hb's on every arm (2.07e-4 to 2.09e-4) [observed]. Clock = our GCD's `average_gfxclk`, card matched by PCI address (address kept on LUMI only).
- **Kept, landed: `HAND_FILL` (unplaced, exact shapes) [observed, 2 opposite-order race jobs plus 2 jobs on the final bytes].** hb's names at the losing shapes show tiles that fill 110 CUs through edge tiles (2816³ MT96x256, 330 blocks; 7168x3584x4096 MT224x96, about 1200; 1024x7680x2048 MT192x128, 320). New `gemm_hand_rows` rounds M, N up to the tile and bounds the buffer resources at the true sizes, so edge rows load zeros with no copy (SMALLM's `rng`). Same-process ratios, race jobs then final-bytes race and module CLI: **2816³ 96x256** 1.028-1.041, 1.015-1.032, 1.008-1.036 (old 128x128 0.979-0.993); **1024x7680x2048 128x192** 1.086-1.098, 1.070-1.092, 1.068-1.094 (old 0.982-0.993); **3072³ 160x192** 1.019-1.031, 1.014-1.035, 1.008-1.032 (old 256x192 0.994-1.015); **7168x3584x4096 160x224** 1.017-1.031, 0.984-1.014, 1.039-1.044 (old 256x128 0.954-0.975). Gain over the old pick in every same-process job [computed]: +2.4 to +5.0%, +8.8 to +10.6%, +1.1 to +2.5%, +2.2 to +6.6%. 7168x3584 is above 1 in 5 of 6 reads, not all; 3072³'s gain is the size of the identical-kernel spread (below). hb's own 96x224 footprint at 7168 reads 0.973-0.990, so fill alone does not decide the tile [observed]. Pad 32 beats pad 16 on every tile where both fit.
- **Kept, landed: range-checked small-M (`SMALL_M_ROWS`) [observed, 2 opposite-order jobs, 4 allocation draws each, then module CLI on final bytes].** Caller's A and B, no A copy, no padded B: **16x17408x5120** 1.009-1.015, 1.010-1.012; CLI 1.012, 1.012 (old 0.988-1.000); **64x5120x5120 32x96** 1.097-1.151; CLI 1.108-1.115 (old 0.970-0.993); **64x5120x17408 32x96** 1.061-1.075; CLI 1.069-1.076 (new coverage, was hb). 32x17408 and 64x17408 tie the padded form, which stays.
- **Bytes.** `gemm_prefetch.py` ecb1cb9d to **31fd591d**, locally and in LUMI `scripts/`; old bytes kept in `evidence/GEMM-MAX/src/` and LUMI `exp/GEMM-MAX/`. CPU check over 12,598 shape and placement cases: dispatch and generated source unchanged everywhere except the 4 HAND_FILL shapes unplaced and the 3 small-M shapes [observed]. `release/` copy stale until `make_release.sh`.
- **Negative: 32x32x8 MFMA in the hand loop [observed].** Numerically correct at once, same registers (152 VGPR + 128 AGPR, no scratch), but 0.84-1.00 of hb at 14 shapes, below land at all 14 by 7.1-14.4%. fr, rdiv, pad 16/48/64 and a row permutation built on a lane-quad bank model all fail to help; that bank model is not supported. x32 ran below land's clock at 11 of 14 shapes and below land's efficiency at all 14 [computed]. hb's 91-94% is not reproduced by the MFMA shape alone; its schedule (PGR2, LDSB1) is the remaining hypothesis.
- **Closed:** fill tiles where we already lead: 8192³ 160x256 pad 16 1.001-1.017 against 1.050-1.067; 16384x4096 +0.3%, +0.6% (under 1%); 32768x1024x2048 96x256 and 160x256 lose 2-6%.
- **Against the ceiling [computed, job f1 plus final jobs].** Rule as landed, % of the clock-scaled MFMA peak (191.5 x clk/1700): 83.5-89.4% at 7 of the 9 established large shapes (8192³ 117.3 TF at 1232 MHz 84.5%, 12288³ 125.1 TF at 1242 MHz 89.4%), 79.0% at 4096³, 73.5% at 32768x1024x2048; 1218-1274 MHz. The best read, 125.1 TF, is 65.3% of the nominal 191.5 TF. hb 78.3-94.5% at 1042-1170 MHz (87-94.5% at the same 7 shapes). New shapes: 1024x7680 74.4%, 2816³ 74.7%, 7168x3584 83%. Small-M is bandwidth bound: 16x17408x5120 0.94 TB/s (59% of 1.6), 64x5120x5120 and 64x5120x17408 0.71-0.74 TB/s (45-46%).
- **Caveat [observed].** At 8192³ the candidate's identical tile and source, compiled as a second code object in the same process, read 1.3% and 2.4% below the landed one. The cause is not traced. It worked against the new arms, but ratios within about 2% are not separable from it. Cap and placement not read for any new entry; placed callers keep the old rule.
- **GPU use [sacct ElapsedRaw, 1 GCD each].** 15 jobs (23566342, 23566628, 23566861, 23567013, 23567014, 23567029, 23567208, 23567209, 23567308, 23567397, 23567398, 23567444, 23567445, 23567449, 23567450): 2206 s = **0.61 GCD-hours**.
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) A general fill rule with edge tiles in `hand_tile`, replacing exact-multiple candidates (needs a fresh shape set read after the fit). (2) A PGR2 or LDSB1-style schedule, first for the 16x16 loop, then for 32x32x8. (3) Fill tiles at the cap and on placed B. (4) Small-M 16x and 1x, which are still near 1.0 or hb.

### 2026-11-09 · DECODE-MAX round 1: balanced and length-aware grids land for 12 decode shapes; the cross-tile prefetch is negative (`evidence/DECODE-MAX/`)
Frame: vLLM 0.30 image, BF16, page 784, LBNHC (TRITON_ATTN), G 6, D 256, seed 988, base/mid/skew lengths (TRI3D generator), every arm replayed from a CUDA graph. Ratios vendor ms over ours, higher is better; gain = landed ms / new ms - 1, same job. HBM % of 1.6 TB/s from the arm's own time. Vendors: Triton `unified_attention` with the 3D arguments serving passes (2D above the threshold, as vLLM does) and ROCM_ATTN's `chunked_prefill_paged_decode` on its own layout. Correct against fp64 on every cell: ours at most 2.22e-3, Triton 2.24e-3, ROCM_ATTN 2.31e-3, 0 NaN.
- **Landed [observed].** `tilelang/gqa_decode.py` and LUMI `scripts/gqa_decode.py` 0603b766 to **67ebe1a8** (old bytes kept in LUMI `exp/DM/gqa_decode_0603b766.py`). `paged_kernels` returns a new form only for BF16, page 784, LBNHC, G 6, D 256, joint stage and (B, MAXP, Hkv) in `_DMAX_BF16_784`; every other call takes the unchanged code. Forms: `decode_partial_paged_v4` (v2/v3 splits, explicit per-thread loads issued before the LDS barrier) at (32,64,4), (64,64,4), (64,128,4), (8,512,2), (64,64,2), (64,128,2) [reverted 9 Nov review: gains not separable from identical-code noise, see the review-fix entry]; `decode_bal` (stream-K: U = Hkv x sum of 16-key tiles laid end to end, fixed grid of 330 blocks = 3 resident x 110 CUs, each takes ceil(U/330) tiles across segment boundaries) at (16,256,2), (16,512,2), (32,512,2), (32,64,2); `decode_seg` (length-aware splits per segment on a fixed grid, tiles per block refined in-kernel so the live blocks fit 330, no segment crossing) at (8,512,4), (16,256,4). Not landed at (8,2048,2) and (32,128,4): no form won all three patterns in both jobs there.
- **Landing race, final bytes, same process old vs new [observed; jobs 23567615 forward, 23567616 and 23567685 reversed].** Of the 36 cells of the 12 table shapes, 35 win in both 615 and 616 (the 36th, Hkv 4 8x8k base, met an external slow period in 616, below) and 35 win in both 615 and 685 (the 36th, (32,64,2) skew, is a tie: +0.83 / +0.39 / -0.12% in 615 / 616 / 685). Largest gains: Hkv 2 16x8k skew +24.8 / +25.4 / +25.7%, 32x8k skew +23.2 / +17.3 / +22.3%, 16x4k base +17.9 / +13.1 / +13.4%; Hkv 4 8x8k skew +17.2 / +17.6 / +18.3%, base +16.7 / +13.7% (616's -64% there is an external slow period, raw kept [corrected 9 Nov review: it did not hit every arm. Cycle 0: newg 1.752 ms slow (first arm tri3g 0.2532 ms after a 454 MHz probe), oldg normal at 0.2436 ms; cycle 1: oldg 0.4514 ms and tri3g 1.154 ms slow, newg normal at 0.2134 ms. With 2 cycles the per-job median is the mean of 2 values and filters no outlier]). Small: the v4 shapes +0.4 to +5.0%, mostly at the noise of the identical-kernel control cells (unchanged shapes, same kernel twice in one process: -0.62 to +1.32%), so the v4 entries are wins by the 2-job rule, not separable from that bias [observed].
- **HBM [computed, min over jobs 615 and 685 per cell].** Hkv 4, 18 cells: lowest 42.4 -> 42.8% (32x2k skew, unchanged shape), median 57.9 -> 60.5%, highest 71.7 -> 72.1%. Hkv 2, 24 cells: lowest 37.0 -> 37.0% (32x1k skew), median 52.0 -> 53.7%, highest 71.9 -> 74.8%. Best new cells 74.8% (Hkv 2 32x8k base), 72.1% (Hkv 4 64x2k base). The 80% goal is not reached; short skew stays at 37-49%.
- **Against vendors [computed, 615 and 685].** Triton as served: Hkv 4 new 0.950-1.276, median 1.148, 4 of 36 cell-job readings below 1 [= 2 cells x 2 jobs] (32x2k skew 0.95-0.96 and 64x1k skew 0.97-0.98, both unchanged forms or v4), landed 0.932-1.235, median 1.044, 8 cell-job readings below 1. Hkv 2 new 0.869-1.275, median 1.095, 2 of 48 cell-job readings below 1 (1 cell in 2 jobs: 8x8k base 0.87-0.89), landed 0.849-1.257, median 1.028, 14 cell-job readings below 1. ROCM_ATTN 2.46-9.80 (Hkv 4), 2.57-24.5 (Hkv 2).
- **Clock [observed, with a caveat].** clock64 ticks over an event-timed 440-block probe run right after each arm: median 935 MHz, range 414-1400 across all arms of 615/685 [corrected 9 Nov review; 428-942 was the new arm only] (low values follow an idle gap, at a cell's first position; the probe runs after the arm, never during it). It reads the GCD just after the arm, not during it [inferred close]. rocm-smi in the job reads 800 MHz and 0% for all devices; amdsmi `current_gfxclk` reads the 1650 target, and polling it from a thread slowed graph replay up to 13x on one cell (dropped); wall_clock64 is not 100 MHz here (ratio about 6080). Decode is bandwidth-bound; the MFMA ceiling does not apply.
- **Levers tried [observed].** L1 cross-tile prefetch (next tile's K/V loads issued after the current tile reaches LDS, same registers): loses in both jobs on 40 of 42 cells, -0.2 to -14% (23567085, 23567086); the ISA has no vmcnt(0) before the barriers, so the loads were in flight across the math, and it is still slower; cause not traced. L1' loads issued before the WAR barrier (v4): +0.2 to +6%, 39 of 42 cells in both of 23567500/501, never a both-job loss except Hkv 2 8x32k base (-0.5, -1.2%), not landed there. L2 V transposed in LDS (32 scalar ds_write_b16 per thread, lanes 0-31 at a 256-byte stride): about 2x slower, one job (23566892), dropped. L3 `decode_bal`: +12 to +35% where the landed split count leaves the grid at 1.5 waves or long splits idle, -11 to -27% at Hkv 4 B 64 (a 44-tile block per 48- or 64-tile segment, so nearly every block crosses a boundary) and -1 to -7% on some mid/skew cells (23567402/403, 500/501). L3b `decode_seg`: no crossing, wins where `bal` lost on Hkv 4 8x8k and 16x4k (all patterns), loses -20 to -34% at B 64 (one segment per block, 64-tile chains). The losses at B 64 for both static forms against the landed 768-block taper suggest the hardware scheduler's dynamic balance over many short blocks beats a static even split there [inferred].
- **Found on the way [observed].** The wheel (no patch 0007) keeps per-thread `bfloat16_t` staging arrays moved by 16-byte struct copies in scratch (144 B/lane, 0 spills; LLVM IR keeps two allocas fed by memcpy); first v4 bytes were 1.6-1.9x slower for that reason. Landed fix: a compile hook `_regvec_edit` rewrites those arrays (only kernels declaring `bfloat16_t Kr[`) as ext_vector uint4 registers, bit-cast at load and LDS store: 0 scratch, v4 157 VGPR + 64 AGPR, occupancy 2. The source was compiled for gfx90a on the login node with the arch forced [corrected 9 Nov review: sacct has no CPU-partition job in round 1, so 'CPU node' here and in NOTES.md was wrong] (`src/dm_dump.py`, `hc.sh`, `asm.sh`), no GPU time. TileLang's runtime-divisor ceildiv emits sign-corrected integer division; `decode_seg` uses an exact fp32 form instead.
- **Correctness details [observed].** Padding rows of length 0 (rows 0 and 3, `padcheck`): live rows within 2.22e-3 on all 14 shapes; `bal` and `seg` give zeros for the empty rows (block 0 writes Cnt 0, combine clamps Cnt to [0, 32]); the v4 and unchanged v2/v3 forms give NaN there, as the landed bytes did before. The vLLM overlay calls `paged_kernels` as k2(*k1(...)), which `bal`/`seg` keep (k1 returns Op, Lse, Cnt).
- **Jobs [sacct, 1 GCD each].** 23566361, 23566892, 23567085, 23567086, 23567138, 23567212, 23567258, 23567330, 23567402, 23567403, 23567447, 23567448, 23567500, 23567501, 23567564, 23567565, 23567615, 23567616, 23567685: 1449 s = **0.40 GCD-hours**. 23567212 aborted in a Triton clock probe (core file deleted), 23567447/448 died at a name clash after measuring one shape.
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) More resident blocks: the wheel reserves 1 KB of reduce workspace that a 64-lane reduce never touches (patch 0006 removes it), so 17408 B LDS caps 128-thread blocks at 3 per CU; 16384 B would allow 4. Needs the reduce done without the workspace in TileLang source, or the fork. (2) A dynamic-balance form for B x Hkv >= 256 and short skew: many short blocks (kk-like) with length-aware boundaries, or `bal` with a work counter instead of a static split. (3) Fuse the combine into k1 (last block per segment) to cut a launch and the Op round trip on 40-100 us cells. (4) Hkv 2 8x8k base, still 0.87-0.89 of Triton: `bal` reaches 1.00-1.04 there but loses mid/skew.

Whole call about 26-27% of 191.5 TF on real FLOPs at T = 2700, k1 about 42% on padded rows [computed]. Skipping MFMAs did not help and prefetch did not help, so k1's limit there is neither MFMA throughput nor simple load latency per step at this occupancy [inferred]; cause open.
- **Open [hypothesis].** Band: direct global->LDS loads (backend gate) and 32x32x8 MFMA (backend), both need a TileLang backend change; zero padding row (`xz_row`, 1-2 points) needs a caller-provided spare row. T = 1: the FullCol + register-prefetch gate/up (`c1d`, graph 0.0700 against vLLM 0.0710 in one job) needs the process-global HIP postproc hook, not landed; eager T = 1 is host-bound (5 launches + allocations). ### 2026-11-09 · PREFILL-MAX round 2: a 32x32x8 form of the d128 kernel (form M) is correct and leads the landed JTP by 1.1-3.7% on three inputs at 8k-16k but ties or loses slightly on `ar`; nothing landed (`evidence/PREFILL-MAX/r2/`, `raw/summary.txt`)
**Not landed: `tilelang/gqa_fa.py` stays e3caf113.** Same setup as round 1: wheel 0.1.15 on the vLLM image (clang 22), CK = flash-attn 2.8.3, B 1, G 6, Hkv 4, D 128, causal; ratio = ours / same-process arm, throughput, higher is better; CK read = mean of pre and post; clock = amdsmi `current_gfxclk` sampled during each timed loop. Ceiling 191.5 TF at 1700 MHz.
- **Form M [observed, compiled].** Hand HIP in `r2/src/m32.py`, compiled through `gqa_fa`'s J stub. Per wave 32 flat rows; S^T = K Q^T with `v_mfma_f32_32x32x8f16` (A = K rows from LDS, B = Q in registers, d permuted so each lane's Q is 128 contiguous bytes); P stays in registers as the B operand of O^T = V^T P^T; one row per lane, so the row max needs one `ds_bpermute` per step and the row sum one after the loop. K in LDS [key][16-byte chunk ^ (key & 7)], V transposed [d][key slot] with chunk ^ (d & 7) and a key-slot order chosen so each A operand pair is one `ds_read_b128`. 0 bank conflicts. M4 = 4 waves, rows 128, 2 blocks per CU; M8 = 8 waves, rows 256. 196-219 VGPRs, no scratch (staging arrays had to be native `ext_vector_type`: HIP's `uint4` union kept them in scratch).
- **Compiler finding [observed].** `__builtin_bit_cast(hv4, v.zw)` on a clang ext-vector swizzle compiled to the low half on clang 22: both MFMAs of each pair read the same registers (`r2/bytes/v4/M4_8192.s`), giving rel_vs_fp64 1.38. Located by a least-squares recovery of the attention weights (keys of odd 8-key blocks missing, even ones doubled; `r2/src/mdiag.py`, jobs 23567614, 23567651, 23567695). `__builtin_shufflevector` halves fix it.
- **Counters, d128 8192, M4 vs CK, rocprofv3 (23567980) [observed].** MFMA instructions 2.54e7 vs 2.56e7 (JTP had 2x), LDS instructions 1.78e7 vs 1.64e7, `SQ_WAIT_INST_LDS` 2.18e7 vs 5.98e6 (JTP 8.5e7), VALU 1.59e8 vs 1.09e8, `GRBM_GUI_ACTIVE` 4.49e6 vs 4.36e6. **Effective clock [inferred]:** GUI_ACTIVE / profiled duration = 1.10 GHz for ours and 1.085 GHz for CK (4.08 and 4.01 ms), and `SQ_VALU_MFMA_BUSY_CYCLES` / (GUI_ACTIVE x 440 SIMDs) = 82% for both. If GRBM counts core cycles, which the two counters agree on, both kernels keep the matrix cores ~82% busy at ~1.1 GHz; the 1650 MHz amdsmi reading is then a target, not the effective clock, and "% at clock" below understates efficiency. Not established for the timed (unprofiled) loops.
- **Races [computed; 0 errors, repeats 20/20, 0 non-finite; rel_vs_fp64 at most 2.74e-4 for the M4HL/M4HXL/M8HXL/P/D arms, = landed; the lazy-rescale Z arms reach 2.84e-4 (M4HLZ and M8HLZ in 23568742/43; M8HLZ 2.65e-4 in 23569376/77), corrected 9 Nov; all far inside the 2e-3 gate; CK 4.7e-4].** M4HL = M4 with address math left to the compiler and K/V loads from a uniform base + 32-bit offset with the V pack by `v_perm_b32` (loop VALU ~450 -> ~350). Over the landed dispatch, 4 pairs of opposite-order jobs (23568542/43, 23568705/07, 23568742/43, 23569376/77), 3-4 inputs: 2048 0.916-0.964 (2 pairs), 4096 0.975-1.009, 6144 0.984-1.020 (2 pairs), 8192 0.988-1.027, 12288 0.994-1.035, 16384 0.996-1.037. By input at 8k-16k: `big` 1.018-1.037 (24 cells), `randn0` 1.011-1.027 (24), `ramp` 1.003-1.017 (6, one pair), **`ar` 0.988-1.005, below 1 in 18 of 24 cells (a tie or a small loss)**; 6144 `ar` 0.984-0.985. Over CK at 16384 0.975-0.999. Caveat (added 9 Nov): these ranges pool 4 job pairs run with 4 different `m32.py` versions (ff3c6abf, 5ad3f0c7, 4113164f, 1c8554db per `r2/notes.txt`); only 1c8554db is kept in `r2/src`, so whether M4HL's bytes were identical across pairs cannot be checked from the evidence. So M4HL is a candidate for L >= 8192 on three of four inputs, not a win on `ar`, and it does not meet the every-cell gate; landing it would need a decision or a fix for `ar` [decision open].
- **Standing, d128 16384 (23569376/77) [computed; corrected 9 Nov]:** means over 6 cells: M4HL 108.2 TF (per cell 100.7-118.5 = 52.6-61.9% nominal), M8HLZ 108.9 TF (102.6-119.1), landed 106.4 TF (98.7-114.7 = 51.5-59.9%), CK 109.7 TF (pre and post reads 101.0-120.4 = 52.8-62.8%). **M8HLZ out-reads M4HL at 16384** (mean 108.9 vs 108.2; M8HLZ/M4HL per cell 0.991-1.020, above 1 in 5 of 6 cells, randn0 1.019-1.020), which the earlier text omitted; by the gate (both jobs above the noise) the best 16k candidate is not separable between M4HL and M8HLZ. The "% at 1650 MHz" figures given before (58.2/57.2/59.0%, and 60-63% at the 1480-1560 MHz readings in 23568742/43) are withdrawn as unreliable: the amdsmi value is the target, not a measured clock. At the inferred effective ~1.1 GHz the same TF would be ~80% of the clock-scaled ceiling [inferred, not established for timed loops].
- **Negatives [computed, same jobs].** M8 (8 waves) below M4 everywhere up to 12288 except `randn0` at 12288 (M8HLZ/M4HL 1.008-1.009); at 16384 M8HLZ/M4HL is 0.991-1.020 (see Standing). exp2 fold (X) 0.3-0.7% below the same arm without it. Form P (QK of step k+1 in the same block as step k's softmax, 256 VGPRs; the compiler interleaves MFMA and `v_exp`): 0.934-0.986 of landed at 4k-16k, every cell; `sched_group_barrier` (P4S) changes nothing. Lazy rescale (Z, max moves by > 8 in log2 units before acc_o is rescaled): +0.3-1% on `randn0`, -0.5-0.7% on `ar`, a tie. Form D (LDS double buffer, 64 KB, 8 waves, one barrier per step): 0.920-0.983 at 4k-8k, 0.972-1.012 at 12k-16k, below M4HL in every cell. Ablation arms with softmax or K/V loads removed (diagnostic, wrong by design) failed to launch ("unspecified launch failure", 23568951, 23569011); cause not found, nothing concluded from them.
- **Not tested.** Clang 18, B > 1, L % 128 != 0, real captured inputs, end to end.
- **GPU use [observed, sacct ElapsedRaw x 1 GCD].** 16 jobs, 1006 s = **0.28 GCD-hours**. Compiles on the login node.
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) Establish the effective clock in timed loops (GUI_ACTIVE with timestamps on unprofiled runs, or a power trace): if ~1.1 GHz holds, the d128 kernels are near 80% of the real ceiling and the remaining lever is energy per flop (fewer LDS and VALU operations per MFMA), not latency hiding. (2) Why M4HL gains least on `ar` (data-dependent power is a guess). (3) A dispatch with M4HL at L >= 8192 if we accept the `ar` tie. (4) pkrtz P cast on form M, after the accuracy decision.

The landed kernel's memory skeleton (`dm2_probe.py` "one": same grid, loads, barriers, LDS store, no attention math; full lengths) reaches 72.7-75.1% at 8x8k/64x2k/32x8k/8x32k and 62.5-67.7% at 32x1k; our full kernels reach 67-75% on the long base cells, so the math is mostly hidden and the remaining gap is the skeleton's [computed]. Note for patch 0006: in the fork it would give these kernels 16384 B and 4 blocks/CU, which loses here; decode then needs an occupancy cap.
- **L2, combine fused into k1 [observed, NEGATIVE].** `decode_f5`: each block fences, thread 0 atomically counts arrivals per (b, hk), the last block combines and resets the counter (graph-safe; correct on every cell). Against `v5p`: mean -9.0%, -23 to -35% at Hkv 2 8x8k/8x32k (one block combines up to 64 splits serially at the tail), -1.5% even at S 4, wins only +0.2 to +2.3% on four B 64 cells. - **L3, paired tile loads [observed, NEGATIVE].** `decode_v6`: K and V of two tiles issued together (32 KB in flight per block, LDS still 16 KB); `amdgpu_waves_per_eu(2)` keeps 186-190 VGPR, 0 spills (195 + 68 AGPR, occupancy 1, without it). Against `v5p`: mean -5.3%, 38 of 42 cells losing in both jobs (23569583/584); at 4 blocks/CU worse still. With round 1's cross-tile prefetch and L1's fourth block, every way to put more bytes in flight per CU has lost [observed]; my inference is that this pattern saturates near the skeleton's 72-75%.
- **L4, uniform split counts for the uniform-path shapes [observed].** S = round(N / (B x Hkv)) for N 330, 440, 660, 990, 1320 against landed (23569694/695): gains up to +25% on a single pattern and losses down to -25% on another; only Hkv 2 32x1k at N 330 won all three patterns in both jobs (landed above). Hkv 2 8x8k, Hkv 4 32x1k, Hkv 2 64x1k: no S wins all three.
- **Tooling [observed].** `_regvec_general` (round-2 v6 only, marker `Vn_s`) generalises the Kr/Vr register edit to any index expression; occ-3 pad needs a read at kernel end or MergeSharedMemoryAllocations reuses it (first try launched with 16384 B).
- **Jobs [sacct, 1 GCD each].** 23568581, 582, 744, 745, 23569087 (cancelled at 18 s, argv bug), 152, 373, 583, 584, 694, 695, 886, 888: 1332 s = **0.37 GCD-hours**; compile checks on CPU debug nodes, no GPU [added 9 Nov review: jobs 23568165 dm2probe FAILED 42 s, 23568418, 23568534, 23568540, 23568636, 23568668, 23568683, 23568713, 23568738, 23569342, 23569538, 23569546, 23569565; 13 jobs, 497 s].
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) A parallel combine that keeps k1's block release fast (e.g. last arrival per segment only marks ready, a small second kernel or the next block reduces), worth up to the 5.7% median combine share. (2) Length-aware split choice inside the kernel for the uniform-path shapes, since the best S changes by pattern by up to 25%. (3) The remaining 5-8 points between the skeleton and streaming need a different memory structure than more bytes in flight (e.g. fewer barriers per byte via a register-only K path into MFMA, skipping LDS for K).

### 2026-11-09 · GEMM-MAX round 3: 8 waves on a range-checked 256x192 tile land at 7 large shapes (+2.0 to +11.7% over the round-2 rule); small-M levers and compiled forms negative (`evidence/GEMM-MAX/RESULTS.md`, round 3)

Over the round-2 rule, race jobs then final bytes: **8192³** +2.8, +3.7, +2.6 / +3.4, +3.3% (CLI 1.074-1.094 of hb); **16384³** +4.9, +4.1, +4.3 / +3.5, +4.4% (1.073-1.087); **7168x3584x4096** +4.1, +4.0, +2.1 / +4.7, +4.7% (1.055-1.066); **2816³** (128x192) +1.9, +1.7, +2.7 / +2.8, +2.0% (1.058-1.072); **16384x4096x4096** +2.5, +1.2 / +2.5, +2.5% (1.063-1.078); **2048³** +11.6, +7.9 / +9.8, +11.7% (1.137-1.175); **4096x17408x5120** (192x256, weakest) +2.6, +2.3, +0.2 / +1.0, +1.4% (1.057-1.073). About a third of each gain is the larger range-checked tile alone (4-wave control on the same tile).
- **Bytes.** `gemm_prefetch.py` 13fc31f1 to **d622fbcc** (= `src/gemm_prefetch_cand4.py`, the raced bytes), locally and in LUMI `scripts/`; old bytes in `evidence/GEMM-MAX/src/` and LUMI `exp/GEMM-MAX/`. CPU check over 12,616 shape and placement cases: dispatch and source unchanged except the 7 unplaced shapes, whose sources equal the raced gp3 sources [observed]. Unchanged shapes read within 0.2% between the modules. `release/` copy stale until `make_release.sh`.
- **Mechanism [observed, inferred].** 8 waves raise MFMA work per clock 2-5 points at every large shape (12288³ 93.0-93.3% against 89.4-90.1%) but the clock drops 30-90 MHz, so on the landed tile they tie (-1.2 to +1.6%). The `gpu_metrics` energy accumulator of our package reads 2.14-2.46e7 units/s across every arm of one job, including hb, while our clock spans 1026-1314 MHz [observed]: a power or current limit sets the clock and TF follows energy per flop [inferred; the package includes a twin GCD another job kept 19-100% busy]. The gain lands where the 256x192 tile also cuts global traffic per flop at fill 0.96-0.98.
- **Negative [observed].** 8 waves on the landed tiles, with PGR2, with other grids (2x4 on 256x192), 192x192, and knobs (swizzle c4/c16/r8, fr 0.35, rdiv 2): ties or losses. New form ties or loses at 12288³, 6144³, 4096³, 5632x3200x4096, 1024x7680x2048, 3072³; +0.7 to +0.9% at 32768x1024x2048 (below the bar). Small M: 32x32 / 64x32 tiles (twice the blocks), PGR 3-4, and 8-wave 64-row tiles: best +0.7% (64x5120x5120), -0.6 to -42% elsewhere. Compiled TileLang (`T.gemm`) with the same footprints at 512 threads: 0.66-0.79 of hb; `select_tile`'s pick 0.85-0.96, against the hand form's 1.078-1.106 in the same job.
- **Contiguity [observed].** At 5 of the 7 entries N rounds up, so `gemm_auto` returns a non-contiguous `[:M, :N]` view (the small-M path already did, e.g. 64x5120x5120). A contiguous form (C allocated (Mt, N), stores past N skipped, `src/gemm_prefetch_cand5.py`) was raced against the landed bytes: -0.6/-0.7% at 8192³, -1.2/-1.3% at 16384³, ties elsewhere; not landed.
- **HBM ceiling, one GCD [observed].** torch `sum` over 1 GB 1.19-1.20 TB/s (75% of 1.6), `copy_` 1.22-1.28 TB/s (76-80%), hb GEMV 0.46-0.74 TB/s. Small-M GEMM (0.72-0.95 TB/s) is at 57-79% of this measured ceiling.
- **Against the ceiling [computed, final-bytes jobs].** Rule as landed: **83.4-91.6% of 191.5 x clk / 1700 at 7 of the 9 established large shapes at 1185-1246 MHz** (round 2: 80.3-89.4%): 16384³ 122.3-123.4 TF 91.0-91.6%, 12288³ 123.9-125.1 TF 90.2-90.5%, 8192³ 119.8-121.4 TF 86.3-89.4%; 4096³ 77.9-78.0%, 32768x1024x2048 73.9-74.0%; best read 125.1 TF = 65.3% of nominal. hb 87.3-94.6% at 1057-1126 MHz. Former losers: 7168x3584 84.9-85.0%, 2816³ 77.2-77.3%, 1024x7680 77.0-77.1%, all above hb (1.055-1.127).
- **Caveats.** Shared nodes; cap (500 W, 8 GCDs) and placed B not read for the new entries; the energy counter's unit was not converted.
- **GPU use [sacct Elapsed, 1 GCD each].** 13 jobs (23569906, 23570072, 23570093, 23570357, 23570549, 23570975, 23570976, 23570991, 23571197 failed in 1 s, 23571226, 23571334, 23571424, 23571425): 3495 s = **0.97 GCD-hours**.
- **Open, by expected size [hypothesis].** (1) Energy per flop is the lever under the power limit: fewer LDS bytes per MFMA (larger per-wave tiles at 4 waves, e.g. 256x256 with an XOR swizzle instead of row padding to fit 64 KiB), or the direct global-to-LDS load path (gfx90a `buffer_load ... lds`, dword only) to drop the staging VGPR write and `ds_write`. (2) A measured per-GCD power trace with the twin idle (an exclusive node) to confirm the limit. (3) K = 2048 and 4096³ shapes (74-78%): persistent tiles overlapping the next prologue with the epilogue. (4) A general rule for the 8-wave 256x192 form (fill and N-rounding based) validated on fresh shapes, rather than exact entries. (5) Small M: ideas tried this round all failed; split-K with a fused reduction is the remaining one.

### 2026-11-09 · GEMM-MAX round 4: XOR-swizzled LDS fits a 256x256 tile; 13 large shapes land, +1.1 to +7.2% solo and +3.2 to +8.8% at the cap; 12288³ reaches 94.6% of the clock-scaled peak (`evidence/GEMM-MAX/RESULTS.md`, round 4)
Ours over hipBLASLt (hb) through torch, by throughput, same process, rotated order, true 2MNK. **Hand asm launched through TileLang, not a compiler result.** Every arm's error against fp64 equals hb's in every job (2.02e-4 to 2.08e-4) [observed]. Clock = our GCD's `average_gfxclk`; % of ceiling = TF / (191.5 x clk / 1700).
- **Kept and landed: XOR-swizzled LDS (`xs` 1, pad 0) [observed; 4 race jobs of both orders, 2 opposite-order final-bytes jobs plus the module CLI, an 8-GCD cap read].** 16-byte chunk c of row r goes to c ^ (r & 7). This is a per-thread constant, so the loop gains no instructions. Without row padding, a **256x256 tile at 4 waves** fits 64 KiB (128x128 per wave, 204 VGPR + 256 AGPR, occupancy 1, no spills), as do 224x256 and 256x224. That means 33% fewer LDS bytes per MFMA than 128x64 per wave. The swizzle also speeds up round 3's 8-wave forms. Generator `gp4.py` (xs 0 = gp3 byte for byte); a CPU address model checks the swizzle.
- **Gains, final bytes (fwd/rev), over the round-3 rule:** 32768x1024x2048 256x256 +7.0/+7.2%; 4096x17408x5120 256x256 +6.5/+6.5%; 4096³ 224x256 +4.4/+4.6%; 12288³ 256x256 +3.5/+3.3%; 5632x3200x4096 256x224 +3.5/+3.4%; 8192³ 256x224 +3.3/+3.3%; 16384x4096 224x256 +3.1/+3.2%; 6144³ 8-wave 256x192 xs +2.6/+2.2%; 16384³ 256x256 +2.3/+1.9%; 2816³ 8-wave 128x192 xs +1.9/+2.2%; 7168x3584 8-wave 256x192 xs +1.9/+1.8%; 3072³ 8-wave 192x256 xs +2.1/+2.2%; 2048³ 8-wave 192x256 xs +1.6/+1.1%. Untried only in the current hand-asm loop: K1 ran the direct load on gfx90a, where register staging beat it by 19-26% at one block per CU and tied at two, and bad offsets fail silently (`writeup/paper-draft.md`, `tilelang/README.md`; corrected in round 5).

### 2026-11-09 · DECODE-MAX review fix: six round-1 v4 entries reverted (inside identical-code noise), (32,64,2) zero-length rows give zeros again, records corrected (`evidence/DECODE-MAX/analysis-r3-review-fix.txt`)
Frame as rounds 1-2 (vLLM 0.30 image, BF16, page 784, LBNHC, G 6, D 256, seed 988, base/mid/skew, every arm graph-replayed, fp64 check on every arm and cell). New harness `src/dm3_race.py` + `src/dm3_job.sh`, analysis `src/cmp3.py` (replaces `cmp.py`, whose 2-cycle median is the mean of 2 and filters no outlier).
- **Harness [observed].** 9 cycles per arm per cell (5 in the smoke and verify jobs), per-job median; arm order rotated by one each cycle (interleaved), the second job reversed. Arms: `old` (0603b766 `paged_kernels`, the pre-DECODE-MAX v2/v3 forms), `ctl` (the same kernel objects in a second graph: identical code), `new` (candidate), at (32,64,2) also `bal` (67ebe1a8) and `u5o` (05f4bbdc), plus Triton 3D as served and ROCM_ATTN. Noise spread of a job = max |ctl/old - 1| over its 21 cells. A cell wins when the gain exceeds the spread in every job; a dispatch entry (3 cells) is kept only if all 3 cells win in both opposite-order jobs.
- **Six v4 entries reverted [observed, jobs 23574143 forward, 23574144 reversed].** Spread 1.75% (one control cell, Hkv 4 64x1k base; the other 20 within 1.09%) and 0.49%. v4 led in all 36 cell-job readings by +0.3 to +2.2%, but no entry won on base, mid and skew in both jobs: (32,64,4) won skew only, (64,64,4) skew only, (64,128,4) none, (8,512,2) mid only, (64,64,2) none, (64,128,2) skew only. All six go back to the 0603b766 code. [inferred] v4 is probably a real gain of about 1%, below what this harness can certify; re-landing needs a tighter noise bound (more jobs or a paired test), not a looser rule.
- **(32,64,2) zero-length rows fixed [observed].** Cause [computed from source]: every split of a length-0 row writes Lse -inf and Op 0, so `decode_combine_v2` computed 0/0. New `decode_combine_v2(..., zero=False)` argument; `zero=True` writes 0 when den is 0 (as `combine_bal` does) and is passed only by the "uS" branch. The default generates the same source as before (`srccheck` in every dm3 job: combine_default_identical true). Padding test (rows 0 and 3 of length 0): new 0 NaN, all zeros, live rows 2.11e-3 against fp64; 05f4bbdc `u5o` 6144 NaN. The reverted shapes keep NaN in padding rows, as the 0603b766 bytes always did (the vLLM overlay copies only output[:n] eagerly and applies nan_to_num when capturing).
- **u5 speed re-checked [observed].** Over `bal`: base +14.3 / +14.7%, mid +9.4 / +10.0%, skew +2.1 / +0.7% (143 / 144), above the spread in both, kept. Cost of the zero fix against `u5o`: -0.2 to -0.45% in all 6 readings, inside the spread.
- **Bytes [observed].** `tilelang/gqa_decode.py` and LUMI `scripts/gqa_decode.py` 05f4bbdc to **b4cfec43** (raced candidate 83ca722a = b4cfec43 before the table edit; old bytes LUMI `exp/DM/gqa_decode_05f4bbdc.py`). `_DMAX_BF16_784` now holds 6 entries: (8,512,4) seg, (16,256,4) seg, (16,256,2) bal, (16,512,2) bal, (32,512,2) bal, (32,64,2) u5. The "v4" branch of `_dmax_kernels` stays, unreachable from the table. The LUMI copy was replaced after the dm3 jobs ended; the jobs then running (PFMAX, MOE-MAX, GEMM-MAX) do not import gqa_decode [observed by grep of their sources].
- **Final-bytes check [observed, job 23574249, 5 cycles].** Reverted shapes: the same error and padding output as old; identical-code readings -1.02 to +0.95% (one beyond that job's 0.95% spread), so even 5- to 9-cycle medians keep tails near 1%. (32,64,2): +15.2 / +8.6 / +1.8% over `bal`. Error against fp64 over the three dm3 jobs: ours (every arm) 2.05e-3 to 2.19e-3, Triton 1.97e-3 to 2.24e-3, ROCM_ATTN 2.10e-3 to 2.28e-3.
- **Standing, final dispatch [computed; 7 re-raced shapes from 143/144, the other 7 shapes from 23569886/888, code unchanged there].** Triton 3D as served over ours: Hkv 4 0.953-1.264, median 1.138, 5 of 36 cell-job readings below 1 in 3 cells (32x2k skew, 64x1k mid and skew); Hkv 2 0.870-1.308, median 1.104, 6 of 48 readings below 1 in 3 cells (8x8k base, 8x32k base and mid). ROCM_ATTN over ours 2.39-9.52 (Hkv 4), 2.51-23.9 (Hkv 2). HBM share (min of jobs per cell): Hkv 4 43.0 / 60.5 / 71.8%, Hkv 2 37.7 / 53.7 / 74.8% (lowest / median / highest).
- **Clock [observed].** Probe right after each arm, never during it: 462-945 MHz (143), 456-1406 (144), 455-945 (249), 457-942 (smoke). Rounds 1 and 2 across all arms: 414-1400 (615/685), 427-1401 (886/888).
- **Record corrections [observed against raw and sacct], made in place in the round 1 and 2 entries and in NOTES.md.** (a) The 616 slowdown did not hit every arm (cycle 0: newg slow, oldg normal; cycle 1: oldg and tri3g slow, newg normal). (b) The round-1 clock range 428-942 was the new arm only; all arms 414-1400. (c) "x of 36 / 48 below 1" counts cell-job readings, not cells (round 1: 2 cells at Hkv 4, 1 at Hkv 2; round 2: 3 and 6 readings). (d) The round-1 gfx90a compile ran on the login node (no CPU-partition job in sacct); "CPU node" was wrong. (e) Round-2 CPU debug jobs listed by id, including 23568165 (dm2probe, FAILED, 42 s). (f) NOTES.md header md5 was stale. (g) Plateau: one criterion from now on. A round is a plateau only if no kept change gains more than the control spread on any cell. Round 1: not a plateau. Round 2 kept +15% at (32,64,2), so it was **not** a plateau either (its plateau=true is withdrawn). This fix round is a review, not a lever round, and claims no plateau. (h) The compile hook `_install_regvec_hook` is process-wide: importing gqa_decode replaces `tilelang.contrib.hipcc.compile_hip` for the whole process. Every later TileLang compile from any module passes through `_regvec_edit`, and it chains with gqa_fa.py's wrapper. The edit changes only source declaring `bfloat16_t Kr[` and raises RuntimeError if such an edit is incomplete; now stated in its docstring.
- **Jobs [sacct, 1 GCD each].** 23574067 (smoke), 23574143, 23574144, 23574249: 463 s = **0.13 GCD-hours**.

The landing race 23567144/45 had no opposite-order pair at all (corrected in the round 1 entry).
- **Rule [fixed before the second pair].** Each race job runs new (landed dispatch), old (the previous dispatch: ffc58f6a's `d128_tile` pick, or the d256 tile without `vt`) and new2 (the same code as new; for JTP the same cached callable). noise(job) = max |new2/new - 1| over all cells of that job. **A length is kept only if new/old > 1 + noise(job) in every cell of every round-3 job that timed it.** Inputs: randn0, big, ar (d128); randn0, self, big, ar (d256).

### 2026-11-09 · PREFILL-MAX rounds 4-5: `pkrtz` P cast (form JTPR) lands in `d128_tile` at all 238 JTP lengths; form M4HL/M4HLR fails against JTPR and is not dispatched; d256 `pk` lost and is removed (`evidence/PREFILL-MAX/r4/`, `r4/raw/notes.txt`)
**Landed: `tilelang/gqa_fa.py` and LUMI `scripts/gqa_fa.py` 98b6e5ec -> bb0808db** (98b6e5ec kept in LUMI `scripts/old/gqa_fa-98b6e5ec.py` and `r4/src/old_98b6e5ec_gqa_fa.py`). Decision: land both M4HL at L >= 8192 and the pkrtz cast, each only where it wins by the rule. Same stack as rounds 1-3 (wheel 0.1.15 on the vLLM image, clang 22, CK flash-attn 2.8.3, same-process throughput ratios, CK = mean of pre and post).
- **What was reused [observed].** Round 4 ran before an interruption: race jobs 23575843 (fwd) and 23575844 (rev), both COMPLETED, 0 errors, 652 rows each (d128), module md5 printed in every job = candidate `49e7e246`; compile job 23575755 from the same bytes. fp64 job 23575845 COMPLETED. 23575846 (d256 `pk` fp64 check) was cancelled once `pk` was out. The earlier session's identity checks of M4HL (`idm.py`) were void: the `.s` files were empty and the JTPR step failed on a cache hit; rerun below for what is landed. All numbers here are recomputed from the raw jsonl (`r4/src/sum5.py`, `r4/src/cktab.py`), not copied from that session.
- **Rule [fixed before the round-4 races, `r4/src/rsum4.py`].** STRICT (pkrtz parts): X/old > 1 + noise(job) in every cell of both jobs. RELAXED (input rule for M4HLR): in each job, every cell X/Y >= 1 - noise(job) and the job mean X/Y > 1 + noise(job). M4HLR at L is kept only if RELAXED holds against old (landed JTP) and, where JTPR is kept, against JTPR too. noise(job) = max |new2/new - 1|, new2 = identical code to new, in one process.
- **Races [computed].** d128, 22 lengths (1024-7168 at 13 points, then every multiple of 1024 from 8192), 4 inputs, 2 jobs, 176 cells. Noise 0.36% (fwd) and 0.63% (rev).
  - **JTPR over JTP: 1.005-1.020, every cell above 1 + noise (min margin +0.11%), at all 22 lengths.** Kept.
  - **M4HLR at 8192-16384, per length** (input cells M4HLR/JTP fwd/rev; M4HLR/JTPR; decision):
    - 8192: JTP ar 0.991/0.993 (loss beyond noise) FAILS; vs JTPR ar 0.982/0.984 fails, mean -0.03/-0.14%. Not dispatched.
    - 9216: JTP ar 0.995/0.996 (0.5% > fwd noise) FAILS; vs JTPR ar 0.986/0.983, mean +0.30/-0.01%. Not dispatched.
    - 10240: JTP ar 0.997/0.998 within noise, mean +1.5% PASSES relaxed vs JTP; vs JTPR ar 0.987/0.984 fails. Not dispatched.
    - 11264, 12288, 13312: JTP ar 0.999-1.001 within noise, mean +1.6-1.8% passes relaxed vs JTP; vs JTPR ar 0.988-0.990 fails. Not dispatched.
    - 14336, 15360, 16384: JTP ar 1.002-1.003 (above 1, below 1 + noise), mean +1.9-2.0% passes relaxed vs JTP; vs JTPR ar 0.986-0.991 fails. Not dispatched.
    - So M4HLR is never the winner over JTPR on all four inputs; `ar` is a real loss (0.9-1.8%, above the noise in both jobs) at every length. By the JTP-only reading of the rule it would be dispatched at 10240-16384 with mean gain over JTPR of +0.2 to +0.9%, at a per-cell `ar` cost against JTPR of 0.86-1.60% at those lengths. The JTPR reference for M4HLR was fixed in the frozen rule before the races (`r4/src/rsum4.py`: RELAXED must also hold against JTPR wherever JTPR is kept), not chosen after them. One can flip this with one line in `d128_tile`; M4HLR stays reachable by an explicit form.
  - **d256 `pk` with `vt`: 0.970-1.003 of `vt` in 72 cells, 3 above 1, none above noise.** Not kept; the `pk` plumbing is removed from the module.
- **Standing vs CK [computed; final dispatch; min-max over 4 inputs, 2 jobs; `r4/raw/ck_table.txt`].** 1024 1.272-1.290, 1280 1.187-1.190, 1600 1.249-1.286, 2048 1.177-1.201, 2240 1.156-1.173, 2560 1.098-1.107, 3072 1.068-1.079, 3584 1.042-1.056, 4096 1.027-1.043, 4608 1.019-1.037, 5120 1.015-1.031, 6144 1.002-1.019, 7168 0.995-1.013, 8192 0.988-1.011, 9216 0.984-1.005, 10240 0.981-1.003, 11264 0.978-0.998, 12288 0.975-0.995, 13312 0.974-0.995, 14336 0.972-0.993, 15360 0.971-0.992, 16384 0.970-0.993. M4HLR at 8192-16384 would read 0.979-1.010 (randn0 1.002-1.010, ramp 0.995-1.005, big 0.982-1.004, ar 0.979-0.992). 1088, 1536 and 3200 keep the previous forms and are not in this round.
- **Dispatch and bytes [observed, CPU job 23579851, `r4/raw/23579851/idf.jsonl`].** Over L 1-20000, B 1 and 2, clang 18 and 22: the final module differs from 98b6e5ec only at clang 22, B 1, d128, 238 lengths (L % 64 == 0, 1024-16384, minus 1088, 1536, 3200), JTP -> JTPR. d256, clang 18, B 2 unchanged.
